# Pulsix v0.2d Notifications & Calendar Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Ship the v0.2d half of the automation design: e-mail and webhook notification channels with a shared delivery queue and log, maintenance windows with the schedules' maintenance guard, schedule notification rules and the weekly digest, runs-list trigger filters, the new activity object types, and the calendar of scheduled runs and maintenance windows. It ends with an E2E in which a failing scheduled run produces exactly one signed webhook message, a maintenance window with `exclude_hosts` leaves a host out of a scheduled run, and the calendar returns the occurrences together with the window.

**Architecture:** A new `internal/notify` package owns channels (config sealed with AAD `channel:<org>:<id>`), a transactional outbox, a delivery queue and the weekly digest. Facts write their deliveries in the same transaction that records them, each in its own savepoint. A finished run group writes them from the job's terminal transaction through a new `jobs.RunGroupSink` port. A skipped, missed or failed schedule decision writes them from the ticker's row savepoint, and the digest from the digest ticker's row savepoint. The unique key `(org, event_key, channel)` makes the enqueue idempotent. A dispatcher claims deliveries with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` and a lease, and sends them through `internal/outbound`, an HTTP client that resolves a destination, dials only the checked address and refuses internal addresses. Delivery is at-least-once. `internal/maintenance` evaluates one-off and recurring windows with `internal/cronexpr`. The schedule ticker consults it for the new `maintenance` guard, which can drop hosts through `jobs.RunRequest.ExcludeHosts`. `internal/calendar` composes schedules, run-group summaries and maintenance occurrences into one range view.

**Tech Stack:** Go 1.27, chi v5, huma v2.39, pgx v5, sqlc 1.31, goose v3, testcontainers-go (TimescaleDB pg18), React 19, TanStack Router/Query, Tailwind v4, Vitest 5. No new dependencies: message rendering uses a small placeholder renderer (changed in Task 2 fix round 1 by user decision), `net/mail` and `crypto/hmac`, and the calendar grid is plain React.

## Global Constraints

- Language: every file in the repository is English. Chat with the user in German.
- Commits: Conventional Commits (`feat`, `fix`, `docs`, `refactor`, `perf`, `test`, `build`, `ci`, `chore`). End every message with the footer `Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>`. Commit only what a task tells you to commit. Work happens on branch `feat/v0.2d-notifications`, and history on `main` stays linear. Never push.
- Go 1.27, module `github.com/noixdev/pulsix`. Code must be `gofmt` and `golangci-lint run` clean. `go test -race -timeout 900s ./...` must be green before every commit. **Every `go test` command in this plan carries `-race -timeout 900s`.** The five pre-existing gofmt deviations of backlog 2.59 stay untouched, and `gofmt -l` must list no file this plan writes.
- IDs are UUIDv7 (`uuid.Must(uuid.NewV7())`, or `uuid.NewV7FromReader(rand)` in services and stores with injected entropy).
- **Layering (CLAUDE.md):** `internal/api` handlers only decode, call a service and encode. Business rules live in domain services that depend on interfaces declared next to them. Stores are sqlc-generated `internal/db/gen` behind a small interface. Handlers never touch `gen.Queries`. There is no package-level state beyond compiled regexps, sentinel errors and constants: fixed lists are functions (`outbound.blockedRanges`, `notify.Categories`), never package variables. Time and randomness are injected. Aim for one responsibility per file; a file over ~400 lines is a signal to split. `internal/jobs/queue_sql.go`, `internal/jobs/service.go` and `internal/schedules/service.go` only gain call sites, interface methods, one-line fields and one-line delegating methods: new logic goes into new files.
- **Migrations are additive only.** The numbers 00028–00030 belong to this plan: `00028_notifications`, `00029_job_trigger_filter`, `00030_maintenance_windows`. Every migration has a working `-- +goose Down`, because `migrations_roundtrip_test.go` rolls back to version 15 and re-applies. The widened `schedule_runs_outcome_check` is dropped and re-added, which relaxes it and deletes nothing, and its Down re-adds the narrow CHECK `NOT VALID`, so a rollback never fails on `skipped_maintenance` rows. New tables need no GRANT, because the default privileges in 00002 cover them.
- Every new org-owned table has `org_id uuid NOT NULL`, `ENABLE` + `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` and the policy pair verbatim: `org_isolation USING (org_id = NULLIF(current_setting('pulsix.org_id', true), '')::uuid)` and `org_isolation_bypass USING (current_setting('pulsix.org_id', true) IS NULL OR current_setting('pulsix.org_id', true) = '')`. `rls_catalog_test.go` finds these tables on its own, and nothing is added to its exemption map.
- References between org-owned rows use composite foreign keys `(org_id, x_id) REFERENCES t(org_id, id)`. That is why a maintenance window has `host_id` and `group_id` columns instead of one polymorphic `target_id` (see "Decisions").
- Stores are built on `*db.DB`. The bypass-scoped paths of this plan run on the raw pool, and each of their statements filters by `org_id` (or by the row's primary key and `org_id`): the dispatcher's claim and outcome writes (`notify.DeliverySQLStore`), the digest ticker's due scan (`notify.DigestSQLStore`), and the decision outbox inside the schedule ticker's existing bypass transaction.
- **Messages never carry log output, variables or secrets.** `notify.Event` has no field for any of them: names, results, counts, durations, generic reasons and links only. A run's `error` text is not copied either, because it can quote the runner.
- Secrets at rest go through `crypto.Box.SealAAD` with AAD `channel:<org>:<id>`: a webhook channel's URL (Slack-style URLs carry their token in the path), every header value and the signing secret. None of them ever appears in an API response (the URL reads as scheme and host, header values not at all), a log line, an audit entry, a delivery row or an error text. `notify.redactURL` strips the URL from transport errors.
- **Every manager-initiated HTTP request to an operator-supplied URL goes through `internal/outbound`.** It resolves first and then dials only the checked address. It refuses the whole destination when any resolved address is loopback, link-local, unspecified, multicast, broadcast or a cloud metadata address, unless `PULSIX_NOTIFY_ALLOW_CIDRS` lists it. It follows no redirects, ignores proxy environment variables and times out after 10 s.
- Every mutating org-scoped HTTP route carries `withAudit(operationID, objectType)`. The new object types are `channel` (channels, Send test, the digest) and `maintenance`. Rule changes are recorded under `schedule`. No route is added to the allowlist in `internal/api/audit_coverage_test.go`.
- Permissions (`internal/tenancy/roles.go`): new `channels.read` (minimum role operator) and `channels.write` (admin), plus `maintenance.read` (viewer) and `maintenance.write` (operator). A schedule's notification rules use `templates.read` / `templates.write` like the schedule itself. The calendar uses `templates.read`. The digest uses `channels.read` / `channels.write`.
- Routes: every new route is org-scoped under `/api/v1/orgs/{org}/...` on the bearer-enabled group: `notification-channels`, `notification-digest`, `maintenance-windows`, `schedules/{id}/notifications` and `calendar`.
- Coverage gates (`scripts/coverage-gate.sh`): every Go package ≥ 80 %. `internal/auth`, `internal/tenancy`, `internal/db`, `internal/ca`, `internal/credentials`, `internal/jobs`, `internal/worker/runner`, `internal/worker/sshprobe`, the inventory packages, `internal/clientip`, `internal/cronexpr` and `internal/enrolltoken` ≥ 90 %. `internal/jobs` stays ≥ 90 % (the dry run measured 90.3 % after Task 5). This plan adds `internal/outbound`, `internal/maintenance` and `internal/calendar` to the 90 % list: they are small, and their code is security- or correctness-critical. `internal/notify` has the default 80 % floor (the dry run measured 87 %). SPA ≥ 70 % (Vitest thresholds unchanged).
- Status colours (`ok #34D399`, `warn #FBBF24`, `crit #F87171`, `unknown #6B7280`) are reserved for states. The accent `#00E5FF` is for actions and selection. Maintenance is drawn in the mockup's violet `#A78BFA`, which is not a state colour. Cron expressions, URLs, header names and ids use JetBrains Mono (`font-mono`).
- **Dependencies are current.** This plan adds none. If an implementer believes one is needed, they stop and ask. The TypeScript 5.9 pin in `docs/dependency-exceptions.md` still applies.
- **Docs:** end-user documentation lives in `~/_dev/noix-docs` on branch `pulsix`, with local commits only, **never pushed**. Public texts never name the tech stack: no Go, Postgres, sqlc, huma, React, "text/template", "SKIP LOCKED" and so on. Words like "webhook", "HMAC-SHA256 signature", "cron expression", "JSON" and "placeholder" are product concepts and allowed. Describe the body template syntax by example (`{{ schedule.name }}`, `{{ run.result | json }}`), never by its origin. Each user-visible task edits its docs page and commits it in the docs repo. The final task reviews them all and updates the changelog.
- The generated SPA client (`web/src/api/schema.d.ts`) is regenerated with `cd web && npm run api:gen` in every task that changes the API (Tasks 4, 5, 7, 8, 9, 10, 11), and committed with it. CI fails on drift.

## Decisions taken in this plan

- **"Run finished" is a run group, reported from its terminal transaction.** A run of a template is one job per zone sharing `run_group_id`. `jobs.SQLQueue.terminate` (every path into a terminal state: the worker's finish, the reaper, the claim-time payload failure, revocation) already takes a per-group advisory lock for rotation groups. It now takes the same lock for `run` jobs when a `RunGroupSink` is wired. Inside that transaction it counts the group, and only the job that makes every job of the group terminal calls `RunGroupFinished`. A job cancelled while still queued does not pass through `terminate` (`CancelJob` is one UPDATE), so `ClearTerminalPayload`, which `Service.Cancel` already calls for exactly that case, re-checks the group in its own transaction under the same lock. Two members finishing together serialize on the lock. If they still both saw completion, the unique event key would collapse the second enqueue.
- **Change detection uses the stored per-host results.** The worker writes `changed_hosts`, `failed_hosts` and `unreachable_hosts` into each job's `stats` (runner `buildStats`), as host ids and as JSON `null` when empty. `RunGroupResult.HostsChanged` counts the distinct `changed_hosts` over the group's jobs. `HostsFailed` counts the distinct union of `failed_hosts` and `unreachable_hosts`. `HostsOK` is the targeted hosts minus the failed ones. A run belongs to `change` when at least one host changed.
- **Categories.** A finished run is `failure` (failed, errored **or cancelled**: it did not do its job) or `success`, plus `change` when a host changed. A decision that started no run is `skipped` (`skipped_overlap`, `skipped_maintenance`, `missed`) or `failure` (`failed`, meaning the run could not be enqueued). A `triggered` decision enqueues nothing itself, because its run reports when it finishes. `Run now` records a triggered decision like the ticker, so its run notifies too, while a failed `Run now` enqueue is answered to the operator directly and not notified.
- **Transactional outbox, one message per run and channel.** `notify.Outbox` inserts deliveries inside the fact's transaction, each call in its own savepoint. A notification failure is logged and loses only the notification, never the fact: a job never stays `running` because of it, and a schedule decision is never re-decided, which would re-run the schedule. Event keys are `run:<group>`, `decision:<schedule run id>`, `digest:<occurrence, UTC>` and `test:<delivery id>`, and `(org_id, event_key, channel_id)` is UNIQUE with `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`. Rules are stored merged per channel (`schedule_notifications`: schedule, channel, `events text[]`). A run therefore yields at most one message per channel however many rules name the channel, and the one message lists every matching category.
- **Delivery is at-least-once, the enqueue exactly-once.** The dispatcher (every 2 s, 8 deliveries per claim, sent in parallel) claims due rows with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, writing a 2-minute lease (`locked_until`) and `attempts + 1` in the same statement. Outcome writes are fenced by `attempts`, so a manager whose lease expired and whose row another manager re-claimed changes nothing. A manager that dies after the receiver accepted a message sends it again once the lease ends. Receivers deduplicate with the stable `X-Pulsix-Delivery` header, and the docs say so. At most `MaxAttempts = 6` attempts, with backoff 30 s × 4ⁿ⁻¹ (30 s, 2 min, 8 min, 32 min, 2 h 8 min), then `failed`. Some failures are permanent and fail at once: a refused destination, a redirect, a 4xx other than 408/429, an instance without mail, and a disabled channel. Each channel keeps its newest 500 finished deliveries, and pending ones are never trimmed.
- **Channels.** `email` sends one message to all recipients (1–20 plain addresses) in one SMTP conversation through the instance SMTP (`mail.SMTP.SendMany`, new, with a 30 s session deadline that now also bounds invitations). Without instance mail an e-mail channel cannot be created (409 with the reason), and the channel list carries `mail_available`. `webhook` takes POST or PUT, a URL (sealed, shown as scheme + host), up to 20 headers (values sealed, hop-by-hop and `X-Pulsix-*` names refused), an optional body template and optional signing. The default body is the event's JSON. A template is literal text with `{{ path }}` / `{{ path | json }}` placeholders from a fixed field allowlist (JSON-escaped; a field absent for an event kind renders empty or `null`; no loops or functions — Task 2 fix round 1, user decision), is at most 16 KiB, renders at most 64 KiB, and is validated at save against a fully populated sample event. Every request carries `X-Pulsix-Event`, `X-Pulsix-Delivery` and `X-Pulsix-Timestamp`. With signing, `X-Pulsix-Signature-256: sha256=<hex HMAC-SHA256 of "<timestamp>.<body>">`, the scheme incoming Pulsix webhooks use. Secrets are write-only: leaving them out of an update keeps the stored values.
- **"Send test" is synchronous.** It sends the sample event (kind `test`) through the real sender right away, records the attempt in the delivery log as `sent` or `failed`, and answers with that row, so the operator sees the endpoint's real answer at once. It is audited.
- **Outbound safety** (`internal/outbound`). The spec's refused set (`169.254.0.0/16`, `fe80::/10`, `127.0.0.0/8`, `::1`) is extended by `0.0.0.0/8` and `::` (they reach the local host), multicast, broadcast, and the two other well-known metadata addresses `100.100.100.200` and `fd00:ec2::254`. A name is refused when **any** of its addresses is refused, so a name cannot resolve to both a public and a loopback address. Private ranges stay allowed. `PULSIX_NOTIFY_ALLOW_CIDRS` re-admits ranges. Redirects are errors, proxy variables are ignored, and every request ends after 10 s.
- **Maintenance windows.** `maintenance_windows` stores `host_id` and `group_id` (composite FKs, `ON DELETE CASCADE`) instead of the spec's single `target_id`, because a polymorphic id can carry no foreign key. The API still speaks `target_kind` + `target_id`. A window is one-off (`starts_at` < `ends_at`, at most 366 days) or recurring: a cron expression evaluated by `internal/cronexpr` in an IANA zone, each occurrence lasting 1 min–744 h in whole minutes. A recurring window is active at `t` when an occurrence started in `(t − duration, t]`. A group window covers the managed hosts of the group and its subgroups, resolved by the same `jobs.ScopeResolver` a run uses, so the guard and the run agree on what a group means. Everyone may read windows, and operators plan them.
- **The maintenance guard.** `schedules.Guards.Maintenance` is `ignore` (the default, and what rows stored before v0.2d read as), `skip` or `exclude_hosts`. It runs after the late/catch-up rule and before the overlap guard. It asks `jobs.Service.RunTargets` for the hosts a run would target and `maintenance.Service.HostsInMaintenance` for those in a window at the decision's `now`. `skip` records `skipped_maintenance` when any of them is in a window. `exclude_hosts` passes them as `RunRequest.ExcludeHosts`, and records `skipped_maintenance` when none would remain: either at the guard, or when the enqueue itself finds every host excluded (`jobs.ErrAllHostsExcluded`, the scope changed in between). A guard that cannot be evaluated records `failed` and never runs (fail closed). `Run now` applies no guard, the same as it already skips the overlap guard. The v0.2c schedules page claims otherwise, and Task 8 corrects it.
- **The weekly digest** is its own ticker in `notify` (every minute, `FOR NO KEY UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` over `notification_digests`, one row per organization), not a second job of the schedule ticker: it has its own table and cadence. The occurrence is `next_run_at`, a weekday and HH:MM in an IANA zone compiled to cron. Each due row is handled in its own savepoint. `schedules.Service.Digest` builds the summary: for every schedule, the runs it started, its failures (failed or cancelled runs plus occurrences that could not start) and its skipped or missed occurrences over the 7 days before the occurrence, plus the 5 slowest finished runs. `Outbox.EnqueueDigest` queues it for the enabled digest channels, and `next_run_at` advances from now. The key `digest:<occurrence>` makes it idempotent per organization and week. A builder failure leaves the row due for the next tick. A digest more than 24 h late (the manager was down) is skipped rather than sent days late. Settings that no longer evaluate disable the digest.
- **The calendar** (`internal/calendar`, `GET /calendar?from&to`, RFC 3339, `to − from` ≤ 42 days) returns entries and maintenance occurrences. Past entries are the `schedule_runs` due in the range, with their outcome and, for a triggered one, the run group's first job and aggregate status (`jobs.Service.GroupSummaries`). Future entries (`planned`) are computed from every active schedule's timetable, strictly after now. A schedule contributes at most 500 and the view at most 5000 entries, and `truncated` says when a cut applied. The SPA draws the mockup's week grid (7 days × 8 three-hour bands, repeated occurrences as `name ×N`, windows as violet bars), a six-week month grid and a list. Clicking an occurrence opens its run, or the schedule when there is none.
- **Runs list and activity.** `GET /jobs` gains `trigger` (validated kind) and `trigger_ref` (a schedule or webhook trigger id), backed by the partial index in 00029. The SPA filters by trigger and, for scheduled runs, by schedule, and shows a Trigger column. The activity page's object-type chips gain `job_template` (scheduled runs are audited against their template, v0.2c), `job`, `schedule`, `trigger`, `worker`, `channel` and `maintenance`.
- **SPA placement.** Channels, their delivery log and the digest live under Settings → **Notifications** (a tab shown to `channels.read`). Maintenance windows are a sidebar entry after Schedules. The schedule panel gains the maintenance select and a channel × category **Notifications** matrix (the stored shape), shown as text to readers. The calendar sits under the schedules list. The mockup's per-row "Notify" column in the schedules list is not built (follow-up 2.73).

## Dependency decisions

None. Everything this plan needs is in the standard library or already in `go.mod` and `web/package.json`:

| Need | Choice | Why not a library |
|---|---|---|
| Webhook body templates | placeholder syntax `{{ path }}` / `{{ path | json }}` over a field allowlist (replaced `text/template` by user decision in Task 2) | a fixed, small struct; validated at save against a sample event |
| E-mail address checks | `net/mail.ParseAddress` | plain addresses only |
| Signing | `crypto/hmac` + `crypto/sha256` | the same scheme as incoming webhooks |
| SSRF-safe dialing | own `internal/outbound` (≈120 lines, 90 % gate) | `http.Transport.DialContext` over a checked `netip.Addr` is all it takes; resolver injected for tests |
| Calendar grid | plain React + Tailwind | three simple layouts; no date library (the browser's `Intl` formats) |

### Task 1: Instance configuration, the outbound HTTP client and bounded mail sends

The groundwork every sender needs (spec §5 "Outbound webhook safety"): `PULSIX_NOTIFY_ALLOW_CIDRS`, the SSRF-safe client in `internal/outbound`, and two additions to `internal/mail`. `SendMany` sends one message to a channel's recipients, and `Configured` answers "does this instance have mail". Every SMTP conversation now also ends at a 30 s deadline (or the caller's, when it is sooner), so a silent server can hold neither an invitation request nor the dispatcher.

**Files:**
- Create: `internal/outbound/policy.go`, `internal/outbound/client.go`
- Test: `internal/outbound/policy_test.go`, `internal/outbound/client_test.go`
- Modify: `internal/config/config.go` (one field, one parse block), `internal/config/config_test.go`
- Modify: `internal/mail/smtp.go` (`SendMany`, `Configured`, context-bound `deliver`)
- Test: `internal/mail/smtp_many_test.go`
- Modify: `internal/api/apitest/apitest.go` (the recording `Mailer` gains `SendMany`, `Configured`, `SetConfigured`)
- Modify: `scripts/coverage-gate.sh` (add `internal/outbound` to the 90 % list)
- Docs (noix-docs): `admin/configuration/environment-variables.md`, `admin/security.md`

**Interfaces:**
- Produces (package `outbound`):
  - `var ErrBlocked = errors.New("destination address is not allowed")`, `var ErrRedirect`
  - `type Policy struct{ /* allow []netip.Prefix */ }`, `func NewPolicy(allow []netip.Prefix) Policy`, `func (p Policy) Check(a netip.Addr) error`
  - `type Resolver interface { LookupNetIP(ctx context.Context, network, host string) ([]netip.Addr, error) }`
  - `const DefaultTimeout = 10 * time.Second`
  - `func NewClient(p Policy, timeout time.Duration, r Resolver) *http.Client` (a zero timeout is `DefaultTimeout`, a nil resolver is `net.DefaultResolver`)
- Produces (package `config`): `Config.NotifyAllowCIDRs []netip.Prefix`.
- Produces (package `mail`): `func (m *SMTP) SendMany(ctx context.Context, to []string, subject, body string) error`, `func (m *SMTP) Configured(ctx context.Context) (bool, error)`.
- Produces (package `apitest`): `(*Mailer).SendMany`, `(*Mailer).Configured`, `(*Mailer).SetConfigured(ok bool)`. The zero value is a configured instance.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing outbound tests**

`internal/outbound/policy_test.go`:

```go
package outbound_test

import (
	"errors"
	"net/netip"
	"testing"

	"github.com/noixdev/pulsix/internal/outbound"
)

func TestPolicyCheck(t *testing.T) {
	cases := []struct {
		addr    string
		allowed bool
	}{
		{"203.0.113.10", true},
		{"10.1.2.3", true},       // private ranges are normal self-hosted receivers
		{"192.168.178.20", true}, // home lab
		{"fd12:3456::1", true},   // unique local IPv6
		{"2001:db8::1", true},
		{"127.0.0.1", false},
		{"127.8.9.10", false},
		{"::1", false},
		{"169.254.169.254", false}, // the classic metadata address
		{"fe80::1", false},
		{"0.0.0.0", false},
		{"::", false},
		{"224.0.0.1", false},
		{"ff02::1", false},
		{"255.255.255.255", false},
		{"100.100.100.200", false},
		{"fd00:ec2::254", false},
		{"::ffff:127.0.0.1", false}, // IPv4-mapped loopback is loopback
	}
	p := outbound.NewPolicy(nil)
	for _, c := range cases {
		err := p.Check(netip.MustParseAddr(c.addr))
		if c.allowed && err != nil {
			t.Errorf("Check(%s) = %v, want allowed", c.addr, err)
		}
		if !c.allowed && !errors.Is(err, outbound.ErrBlocked) {
			t.Errorf("Check(%s) = %v, want ErrBlocked", c.addr, err)
		}
	}
}

func TestPolicyAllowlistReadmitsBlockedRanges(t *testing.T) {
	p := outbound.NewPolicy([]netip.Prefix{netip.MustParsePrefix("127.0.0.0/8")})
	if err := p.Check(netip.MustParseAddr("127.0.0.1")); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("allowlisted loopback refused: %v", err)
	}
	if err := p.Check(netip.MustParseAddr("169.254.169.254")); !errors.Is(err, outbound.ErrBlocked) {
		t.Fatalf("metadata outside the allowlist = %v, want ErrBlocked", err)
	}
	if err := p.Check(netip.Addr{}); !errors.Is(err, outbound.ErrBlocked) {
		t.Fatalf("invalid address = %v, want ErrBlocked", err)
	}
}

func TestBlockedErrorNamesAddressAndReason(t *testing.T) {
	err := outbound.NewPolicy(nil).Check(netip.MustParseAddr("169.254.169.254"))
	if err == nil || err.Error() != "destination address is not allowed: 169.254.169.254 is link-local (allow it with PULSIX_NOTIFY_ALLOW_CIDRS)" {
		t.Fatalf("error = %v", err)
	}
}
```

`internal/outbound/client_test.go`:

```go
package outbound_test

import (
	"context"
	"errors"
	"net/http"
	"net/http/httptest"
	"net/netip"
	"net/url"
	"strings"
	"sync/atomic"
	"testing"
	"time"

	"github.com/noixdev/pulsix/internal/outbound"
)

// fakeResolver answers every name with fixed addresses and counts lookups.
type fakeResolver struct {
	addrs   []netip.Addr
	err     error
	lookups atomic.Int32
}

func (f *fakeResolver) LookupNetIP(context.Context, string, string) ([]netip.Addr, error) {
	f.lookups.Add(1)
	return f.addrs, f.err
}

func loopbackOnly() outbound.Policy {
	return outbound.NewPolicy([]netip.Prefix{netip.MustParsePrefix("127.0.0.0/8"), netip.MustParsePrefix("::1/128")})
}

func TestClientDialsTheResolvedAddress(t *testing.T) {
	srv := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
		if !strings.HasPrefix(r.Host, "hooks.example.test:") {
			t.Errorf("Host header = %q, want the URL's name", r.Host)
		}
		w.WriteHeader(http.StatusNoContent)
	}))
	defer srv.Close()
	port := srv.URL[strings.LastIndex(srv.URL, ":")+1:]
	r := &fakeResolver{addrs: []netip.Addr{netip.MustParseAddr("127.0.0.1")}}
	resp, err := outbound.NewClient(loopbackOnly(), time.Second, r).Get("http://hooks.example.test:" + port + "/x")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	_ = resp.Body.Close()
	if resp.StatusCode != http.StatusNoContent || r.lookups.Load() != 1 {
		t.Fatalf("status %d, lookups %d", resp.StatusCode, r.lookups.Load())
	}
}

func TestClientRefusesBlockedDestinations(t *testing.T) {
	cases := []struct {
		name     string
		url      string
		resolver *fakeResolver
	}{
		{"loopback literal", "http://127.0.0.1:9/", &fakeResolver{}},
		{"metadata literal", "http://169.254.169.254/latest/meta-data", &fakeResolver{}},
		{"name resolving to loopback", "http://evil.example.test/", &fakeResolver{addrs: []netip.Addr{netip.MustParseAddr("127.0.0.1")}}},
		{"any blocked answer refuses the name", "http://mixed.example.test/", &fakeResolver{addrs: []netip.Addr{netip.MustParseAddr("203.0.113.5"), netip.MustParseAddr("169.254.169.254")}}},
		{"ipv6 loopback literal", "http://[::1]:9/", &fakeResolver{}},
	}
	for _, c := range cases {
		t.Run(c.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			_, err := outbound.NewClient(outbound.NewPolicy(nil), time.Second, c.resolver).Get(c.url)
			if !errors.Is(err, outbound.ErrBlocked) {
				t.Fatalf("Get(%s) = %v, want ErrBlocked", c.url, err)
			}
		})
	}
}

func TestClientDoesNotFollowRedirects(t *testing.T) {
	var hits atomic.Int32
	srv := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
		hits.Add(1)
		http.Redirect(w, r, "/elsewhere", http.StatusFound)
	}))
	defer srv.Close()
	_, err := outbound.NewClient(loopbackOnly(), time.Second, nil).Get(srv.URL)
	if !errors.Is(err, outbound.ErrRedirect) || hits.Load() != 1 {
		t.Fatalf("err = %v, hits = %d; want ErrRedirect after one request", err, hits.Load())
	}
}

func TestClientTimesOut(t *testing.T) {
	release := make(chan struct{})
	srv := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
		<-release
	}))
	defer srv.Close()
	defer close(release)
	start := time.Now()
	_, err := outbound.NewClient(loopbackOnly(), 200*time.Millisecond, nil).Get(srv.URL)
	var ue *url.Error
	if !errors.As(err, &ue) || !ue.Timeout() || time.Since(start) > 5*time.Second {
		t.Fatalf("err = %v after %s, want a timeout", err, time.Since(start))
	}
}

func TestClientIgnoresProxyEnvironment(t *testing.T) {
	t.Setenv("HTTP_PROXY", "http://127.0.0.1:1")
	srv := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) { w.WriteHeader(http.StatusOK) }))
	defer srv.Close()
	resp, err := outbound.NewClient(loopbackOnly(), time.Second, nil).Get(srv.URL)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("a proxy variable must not reroute the request: %v", err)
	}
	_ = resp.Body.Close()
}

func TestClientReportsResolverFailures(t *testing.T) {
	r := &fakeResolver{err: errors.New("no such host")}
	if _, err := outbound.NewClient(outbound.NewPolicy(nil), time.Second, r).Get("http://unknown.example.test/"); err == nil || !strings.Contains(err.Error(), "no such host") {
		t.Fatalf("err = %v", err)
	}
	empty := &fakeResolver{}
	if _, err := outbound.NewClient(outbound.NewPolicy(nil), time.Second, empty).Get("http://empty.example.test/"); err == nil || !strings.Contains(err.Error(), "no addresses") {
		t.Fatalf("err = %v", err)
	}
}

func TestNewClientDefaultsTheTimeout(t *testing.T) {
	if c := outbound.NewClient(outbound.NewPolicy(nil), 0, nil); c.Timeout != outbound.DefaultTimeout {
		t.Fatalf("timeout = %s", c.Timeout)
	}
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement the package**

`internal/outbound/policy.go`:

```go
// Package outbound is the HTTP client every manager-initiated request to
// an operator-supplied URL goes through (notification webhooks, spec
// 2026-09-18 §5 "Outbound webhook safety"): the destination name is
// resolved first and the connection is dialled to the checked address, so
// a DNS answer cannot change between the check and the dial; loopback,
// link-local, unspecified, multicast and cloud metadata addresses are
// refused unless PULSIX_NOTIFY_ALLOW_CIDRS lists them; redirects are never
// followed; every request ends after a fixed timeout. Private ranges
// (10/8, 172.16/12, 192.168/16, fc00::/7) are allowed: self-hosted
// receivers are the normal case.
package outbound

import (
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"net/netip"
)

// ErrBlocked is the refusal of a destination address. The error text
// names the address and why, never the URL (URLs can carry secrets).
var ErrBlocked = errors.New("destination address is not allowed")

// blockedRange is one refused range and the reason shown to operators.
type blockedRange struct {
	prefix netip.Prefix
	reason string
}

// blockedRanges lists the refused ranges. It is a function, not a
// package variable, so nothing can change the list at run time.
func blockedRanges() []blockedRange {
	return []blockedRange{
		{netip.MustParsePrefix("127.0.0.0/8"), "loopback"},
		{netip.MustParsePrefix("::1/128"), "loopback"},
		{netip.MustParsePrefix("169.254.0.0/16"), "link-local"},
		{netip.MustParsePrefix("fe80::/10"), "link-local"},
		{netip.MustParsePrefix("0.0.0.0/8"), "unspecified"},
		{netip.MustParsePrefix("::/128"), "unspecified"},
		{netip.MustParsePrefix("224.0.0.0/4"), "multicast"},
		{netip.MustParsePrefix("ff00::/8"), "multicast"},
		{netip.MustParsePrefix("255.255.255.255/32"), "broadcast"},
		{netip.MustParsePrefix("100.100.100.200/32"), "cloud metadata"},
		{netip.MustParsePrefix("fd00:ec2::254/128"), "cloud metadata"},
	}
}

// Policy decides which destination addresses may be dialled. The zero
// value refuses every blocked range and allows everything else.
type Policy struct {
	allow []netip.Prefix
}

// NewPolicy returns a Policy whose allowlist (PULSIX_NOTIFY_ALLOW_CIDRS)
// re-admits addresses inside the blocked ranges.
func NewPolicy(allow []netip.Prefix) Policy {
	return Policy{allow: append([]netip.Prefix(nil), allow...)}
}

// Check reports whether a may be dialled: nil, or an error wrapping
// ErrBlocked. IPv4-mapped IPv6 addresses are judged as IPv4.
func (p Policy) Check(a netip.Addr) error {
	a = a.Unmap()
	if !a.IsValid() {
		return fmt.Errorf("%w: invalid address", ErrBlocked)
	}
	for _, pre := range p.allow {
		if pre.Contains(a) {
			return nil
		}
	}
	for _, b := range blockedRanges() {
		if b.prefix.Contains(a) {
			return fmt.Errorf("%w: %s is %s (allow it with PULSIX_NOTIFY_ALLOW_CIDRS)", ErrBlocked, a, b.reason)
		}
	}
	return nil
}
```

`internal/outbound/client.go`:

```go
package outbound

import (
	"context"
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"net"
	"net/http"
	"net/netip"
	"strconv"
	"time"
)

// DefaultTimeout bounds one request from dial to the end of the body.
const DefaultTimeout = 10 * time.Second

// ErrRedirect is returned for any 3xx: redirects are never followed, the
// response is a failed delivery.
var ErrRedirect = errors.New("the endpoint answered with a redirect, which is not followed")

// Resolver looks names up; *net.Resolver implements it.
type Resolver interface {
	LookupNetIP(ctx context.Context, network, host string) ([]netip.Addr, error)
}

// NewClient returns an http.Client that dials only addresses p admits,
// never follows redirects, ignores proxy environment variables (a proxy
// would dial on our behalf, past the check) and gives up after timeout
// (DefaultTimeout when zero). r nil means net.DefaultResolver.
func NewClient(p Policy, timeout time.Duration, r Resolver) *http.Client {
	if timeout <= 0 {
		timeout = DefaultTimeout
	}
	if r == nil {
		r = net.DefaultResolver
	}
	d := &dialer{policy: p, resolver: r, net: &net.Dialer{Timeout: timeout}}
	transport := &http.Transport{
		Proxy:                 nil,
		DialContext:           d.DialContext,
		ForceAttemptHTTP2:     true,
		MaxIdleConns:          10,
		IdleConnTimeout:       30 * time.Second,
		TLSHandshakeTimeout:   timeout,
		ResponseHeaderTimeout: timeout,
	}
	return &http.Client{
		Transport: transport,
		Timeout:   timeout,
		CheckRedirect: func(*http.Request, []*http.Request) error {
			return ErrRedirect
		},
	}
}

type dialer struct {
	policy   Policy
	resolver Resolver
	net      *net.Dialer
}

// DialContext resolves host, refuses the destination when ANY resolved
// address is blocked (a name that also points at loopback is not a
// receiver worth trusting), and dials the first checked address itself —
// never the name, so a second DNS answer cannot slip in.
func (d *dialer) DialContext(ctx context.Context, network, addr string) (net.Conn, error) {
	host, portStr, err := net.SplitHostPort(addr)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	port, err := strconv.ParseUint(portStr, 10, 16)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("invalid port %q", portStr)
	}
	var addrs []netip.Addr
	if a, perr := netip.ParseAddr(host); perr == nil {
		addrs = []netip.Addr{a}
	} else {
		addrs, err = d.resolver.LookupNetIP(ctx, "ip", host)
		if err != nil {
			return nil, fmt.Errorf("resolve %s: %w", host, err)
		}
		if len(addrs) == 0 {
			return nil, fmt.Errorf("resolve %s: no addresses", host)
		}
	}
	for _, a := range addrs {
		if err := d.policy.Check(a); err != nil {
			return nil, err
		}
	}
	target := netip.AddrPortFrom(addrs[0].Unmap(), uint16(port))
	return d.net.DialContext(ctx, network, target.String())
}
```

```go
package mail_test

import (

func TestSendManyDeliversOneMessageToEveryRecipient(t *testing.T) {
	backend := &testBackend{username: "pulsix", password: "correct horse"}
	host, port := startTestServer(t, backend, true)
	m := mail.NewSMTP(func(context.Context) (mail.SMTPSettings, error) {
		return mail.SMTPSettings{Host: host, Port: port, Username: "pulsix", Password: "correct horse", From: "pulsix@example.test"}, nil
	})
	if err := m.SendMany(context.Background(), []string{"ops@example.test", "dba@example.test"}, "Run failed", "body"); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	got := backend.recorded()
	if len(got) != 1 || len(got[0].to) != 2 || got[0].to[1] != "dba@example.test" {
		t.Fatalf("recorded = %+v, want one message with two recipients", got)
	}
	if !strings.Contains(string(got[0].data), "To: ops@example.test, dba@example.test\r\n") {
		t.Fatalf("To header missing: %s", got[0].data)
	}
}

func TestSendManyRefusesNoRecipientsAndLineBreaks(t *testing.T) {
	m := mail.NewSMTP(func(context.Context) (mail.SMTPSettings, error) {
		return mail.SMTPSettings{Host: "127.0.0.1", Port: 1, From: "pulsix@example.test"}, nil
	})
	if err := m.SendMany(context.Background(), nil, "s", "b"); err == nil {
		t.Fatal("no recipients accepted")
	}
	if err := m.SendMany(context.Background(), []string{"a@example.test", "b@example.test\r\nBcc: x@example.test"}, "s", "b"); err == nil || !strings.Contains(err.Error(), "line break") {
		t.Fatalf("err = %v, want the line-break refusal", err)
	}
	failing := mail.NewSMTP(func(context.Context) (mail.SMTPSettings, error) { return mail.SMTPSettings{}, errors.New("db down") })
	if err := failing.SendMany(context.Background(), []string{"a@example.test"}, "s", "b"); err == nil || !strings.Contains(err.Error(), "db down") {
		t.Fatalf("loader error = %v", err)
	}
}

func TestConfigured(t *testing.T) {
	cases := []struct {
		name    string
		load    func(context.Context) (mail.SMTPSettings, error)
		want    bool
		wantErr bool
	}{
		{"stored", func(context.Context) (mail.SMTPSettings, error) {
			return mail.SMTPSettings{Host: "smtp.example.test"}, nil
		}, true, false},
		{"nothing stored", func(context.Context) (mail.SMTPSettings, error) {
			return mail.SMTPSettings{}, fmt.Errorf("not found: %w", mail.ErrNotConfigured)
		}, false, false},
		{"empty host", func(context.Context) (mail.SMTPSettings, error) { return mail.SMTPSettings{}, nil }, false, false},
		{"loader failure", func(context.Context) (mail.SMTPSettings, error) { return mail.SMTPSettings{}, errors.New("db down") }, false, true},
	}
	for _, c := range cases {
		got, err := mail.NewSMTP(c.load).Configured(context.Background())
		if got != c.want || (err != nil) != c.wantErr {
			t.Errorf("%s: Configured = %v, %v", c.name, got, err)
		}
	}
}

// TestSendGivesUpOnASilentServer: a server that accepts the connection and
// never greets must not hold the caller past its context.
func TestSendGivesUpOnASilentServer(t *testing.T) {
	ln, err := net.Listen("tcp", "127.0.0.1:0")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	defer ln.Close() //nolint:errcheck
	accepted := make(chan net.Conn, 1)
	go func() {
		if c, err := ln.Accept(); err == nil {
			accepted <- c
		}
	}()
	defer func() {
		select {
		case c := <-accepted:
			_ = c.Close()
		default:
		}
	}()
	addr := ln.Addr().(*net.TCPAddr)
	m := mail.NewSMTP(func(context.Context) (mail.SMTPSettings, error) {
		return mail.SMTPSettings{Host: "127.0.0.1", Port: addr.Port, From: "pulsix@example.test"}, nil
	})
	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 300*time.Millisecond)
	defer cancel()
	start := time.Now()
	if err := m.Send(ctx, "dest@example.test", "s", "b"); err == nil {
		t.Fatal("a silent server accepted the message")
	}
	if elapsed := time.Since(start); elapsed > 5*time.Second {
		t.Fatalf("Send took %s against a silent server", elapsed)
	}
}
```

- [ ] **Step 7: Implement `SendMany`, `Configured` and the session deadline**

In `internal/mail/smtp.go`, add `"time"` to the imports and, after the import block:

```go
// sessionTimeout bounds one whole SMTP conversation, from the dial to
// QUIT, unless the caller's context ends it sooner. Without it a server
// that accepts the connection and never answers would hold the caller —
// an invitation request or a notification dispatcher — forever.
const sessionTimeout = 30 * time.Second
```

In `Send`, replace `return deliver(settings, to, subject, body)` with `return deliver(ctx, settings, []string{to}, subject, body)`, and in `Test` replace `return deliver(settings, to, "Pulsix test e-mail", …)` with `return deliver(ctx, settings, []string{to}, "Pulsix test e-mail", …)` (message text unchanged). After `Send`, add:

```go
// SendMany delivers one message to several recipients in one SMTP
// conversation (every address is a RCPT TO and listed in the To header).
// A notification channel's recipients share the message, the way a
// mailing list does.
func (m *SMTP) SendMany(ctx context.Context, to []string, subject, body string) error {
	if len(to) == 0 {
		return errors.New("smtp: no recipients")
	}
	settings, err := m.load(ctx)
	if err != nil {
		return fmt.Errorf("load smtp settings: %w", err)
	}
	return deliver(ctx, settings, to, subject, body)
}

// Configured reports whether the instance has outbound mail settings. A
// loader error other than "nothing stored" is returned as is.
func (m *SMTP) Configured(ctx context.Context) (bool, error) {
	settings, err := m.load(ctx)
	if errors.Is(err, ErrNotConfigured) {
		return false, nil
	}
	if err != nil {
		return false, fmt.Errorf("load smtp settings: %w", err)
	}
	return settings.Host != "", nil
}
```

Replace the head of `deliver`, from its signature through `defer c.Close() //nolint:errcheck`:

```go
func deliver(settings SMTPSettings, to, subject, body string) error {
	if settings.Host == "" {
		return ErrNotConfigured
	}
	if strings.ContainsAny(settings.From, "\r\n") || strings.ContainsAny(to, "\r\n") {
		return errHeaderInjection
	}
	addr := net.JoinHostPort(settings.Host, strconv.Itoa(settings.Port))
	c, err := smtp.Dial(addr)
	if err != nil {
		return fmt.Errorf("dial %s: %w", addr, err)
	}
	defer c.Close() //nolint:errcheck
```

with:

```go
func deliver(ctx context.Context, settings SMTPSettings, to []string, subject, body string) error {
	if settings.Host == "" {
		return ErrNotConfigured
	}
	if strings.ContainsAny(settings.From, "\r\n") {
		return errHeaderInjection
	}
	for _, rcpt := range to {
		if strings.ContainsAny(rcpt, "\r\n") {
			return errHeaderInjection
		}
	}
	deadline := time.Now().Add(sessionTimeout)
	if d, ok := ctx.Deadline(); ok && d.Before(deadline) {
		deadline = d
	}
	dctx, cancel := context.WithDeadline(ctx, deadline)
	defer cancel()
	addr := net.JoinHostPort(settings.Host, strconv.Itoa(settings.Port))
	conn, err := (&net.Dialer{}).DialContext(dctx, "tcp", addr)
	if err != nil {
		return fmt.Errorf("dial %s: %w", addr, err)
	}
	// Every later read and write of the conversation shares the deadline.
	if err := conn.SetDeadline(deadline); err != nil {
		_ = conn.Close()
		return fmt.Errorf("set smtp deadline: %w", err)
	}
	c, err := smtp.NewClient(conn, settings.Host)
	if err != nil {
		_ = conn.Close()
		return fmt.Errorf("smtp greeting from %s: %w", addr, err)
	}
	defer c.Close() //nolint:errcheck
```

```go
		return fmt.Errorf("rcpt to: %w", err)
	}
```

with

```go
	}
```

and `buildMessage(settings.From, to, subject, body)` with `buildMessage(settings.From, strings.Join(to, ", "), subject, body)`. Expected: PASS (the existing tests unchanged; the dry run measured 89.5 %).

- [ ] **Step 8: The recording mailer**

In `internal/api/apitest/apitest.go`, add `"strings"` to the imports and replace the `Mailer` struct with:

```go
type Mailer struct {
	mu   sync.Mutex
	sent []SentMail
	fail error
	// unconfigured makes Configured report an instance without outgoing
	// mail (SetConfigured); the zero value is a configured instance.
	unconfigured bool
}

// SendMany records one message addressed to every recipient (To joined
// with ", "), like mail.SMTP.SendMany sends one.
func (m *Mailer) SendMany(ctx context.Context, to []string, subject, body string) error {
	return m.Send(ctx, strings.Join(to, ", "), subject, body)
}

// Configured reports whether the fake instance has outgoing mail.
func (m *Mailer) Configured(context.Context) (bool, error) {
	m.mu.Lock()
	defer m.mu.Unlock()
	return !m.unconfigured, nil
}

// SetConfigured switches the fake instance's outgoing mail on or off.
func (m *Mailer) SetConfigured(ok bool) {
	m.mu.Lock()
	defer m.mu.Unlock()
	m.unconfigured = !ok
}
```

- [ ] **Step 10: Docs, lint and commit**

In `~/_dev/noix-docs/src/content/docs/pulsix/admin/configuration/environment-variables.md`, add a row after `PULSIX_TRUSTED_PROXIES`:

```markdown
| `PULSIX_NOTIFY_ALLOW_CIDRS` | no | — | Comma-separated addresses or networks that notification webhooks may reach although they are normally refused: the manager itself (`127.0.0.0/8`, `::1`), link-local and cloud metadata addresses. Private networks such as `10.0.0.0/8` or `192.168.0.0/16` need no entry. Leave it empty unless a webhook receiver runs on the manager's own host |
```

In `admin/security.md`, add a section before "## Public status pages":

```markdown
## Outgoing notifications

Notification webhooks are requests Pulsix makes to addresses your team enters, so
they are checked before they are sent:

- The receiver's name is looked up first, and Pulsix connects only to the address it
  checked — a second lookup cannot redirect the request.
- The manager's own host, link-local addresses and cloud metadata services are never
  contacted (and a name that resolves to one of them is refused as a whole), unless
  an administrator allows the range with `PULSIX_NOTIFY_ALLOW_CIDRS`. Private networks
  are allowed: self-hosted receivers are the normal case.
- Redirects are not followed, and every request gives up after 10 seconds.
- A webhook's URL, its header values and its signing secret are stored encrypted and
  never shown again; the channel list shows only the URL's scheme and host.
- Messages carry names, results, counts, durations and links — never run output,
  variables or secrets.
```

### Task 2: Notification channels (migration 00028, validation, sealing, store, service)

This task covers spec §5's `notification_channels`. The migration creates **every** notification table at once: channels, deliveries, `schedule_notifications` and the digest tables, so the schema has one coherent version. Later tasks only add queries. The channel domain validates and seals a channel's settings and hands out a secret-free view. The event model and its rendering live here too, because a webhook body template is validated when the channel is saved.

**Files:**
- Create: `internal/db/migrations/00028_notifications.sql`, `internal/db/queries/notifications.sql`
- Create: `internal/notify/channel.go`, `validate.go`, `event.go`, `render.go`, `service.go`, `store_sql.go`
- Test: `internal/notify/validate_test.go`, `render_test.go`, `service_test.go`, `store_sql_test.go`
- Modify: `internal/tenancy/roles.go`, `internal/tenancy/roles_test.go` (`channels.read`, `channels.write`)
- Regenerate: `internal/db/gen` (`make sqlc`)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `crypto.Box.SealAAD/OpenAAD` (as `notify.Sealer`), `mail.SMTP.Configured` (Task 1, as part of `notify.Mailer`).
- Produces (package `notify`):
  - sentinels `ErrNotFound`, `ErrInvalidInput`, `ErrNameTaken`, `ErrRevisionMismatch`, `ErrMailUnavailable`
  - `type Kind string` (`KindEmail`, `KindWebhook`); `type Channel struct{ ID, OrgID uuid.UUID; Kind Kind; Name string; Enabled bool; Config json.RawMessage; CreatedBy *uuid.UUID; CreatedAt, UpdatedAt time.Time; Revision int64 }`; `func (c Channel) View() (*EmailConfig, *WebhookView, error)`
  - `type EmailConfig struct{ Recipients []string }`; `type WebhookView struct{ Method, URLDisplay string; HeaderNames []string; BodyTemplate string; Signed bool }`
  - `type Input struct{ Kind Kind; Name string; Enabled bool; Email *EmailConfig; Webhook *WebhookInput }`; `type WebhookInput struct{ Method string; URL *string; Headers []HeaderInput; BodyTemplate string; Signed bool; SigningSecret *string }`; `type HeaderInput struct{ Name string; Value *string }`
  - `func AAD(orgID, id uuid.UUID) []byte` (`channel:<org>:<id>`)
  - `type Category string` (`CategoryFailure`, `CategoryChange`, `CategorySuccess`, `CategorySkipped`), `func Categories() []Category`, `func ParseCategory(s string) (Category, bool)`
  - `type EventKind string` (`EventRunFinished` `run.finished`, `EventScheduleSkipped` `schedule.skipped`, `EventScheduleFailed` `schedule.failed`, `EventDigest` `digest.weekly`, `EventTest` `test`)
  - `type Event struct{ Kind; At; Org OrgRef; Categories []Category; Schedule, Template *NamedRef; Run *RunSummary; Decision *DecisionSummary; Digest *DigestSummary; URL string }` and its sections `OrgRef`, `NamedRef`, `RunSummary`, `DecisionSummary`, `DigestSummary`, `DigestSchedule`, `DigestRun` (fields exactly as in `event.go` below)
  - unexported, used by later tasks: `renderWebhookBody`, `renderEmail`, `sampleEvent`, `runCategories`, `decisionCategory`, `errorText` (Task 3)
  - `type ChannelStore interface{ CreateChannel; GetChannel; ListChannels; UpdateChannel; DeleteChannel }`, `type Sealer`, `type Mailer interface{ SendMany(ctx, to []string, subject, body string) error; Configured(ctx) (bool, error) }`
  - `type Deps struct{ Channels ChannelStore; Box Sealer; Mail Mailer; Now func() time.Time; Rand io.Reader; Log *slog.Logger }` (Tasks 3, 9 and 10 add fields), `func NewService(d Deps) *Service`
  - `Service.CreateChannel(ctx, orgID, actor uuid.UUID, in Input) (Channel, error)`, `GetChannel(ctx, orgID, id)`, `ListChannels(ctx, orgID)`, `UpdateChannel(ctx, orgID, id uuid.UUID, revision int64, in Input)`, `DeleteChannel(ctx, orgID, id)`, `MailAvailable(ctx) (bool, error)`
  - `type SQLStore`, `func NewSQLStore(d *db.DB) *SQLStore` (implements `ChannelStore`)
- Produces (package `tenancy`): `PermChannelsRead` (`channels.read`, operator), `PermChannelsWrite` (`channels.write`, admin).

- [ ] **Step 1: The migration**

`internal/db/migrations/00028_notifications.sql`:

```sql
-- +goose Up
-- Notification channels, their delivery queue and log, schedule notification
-- rules and the weekly digest (spec 2026-09-18 §5, §7). v0.4 extends the
-- same tables with more channel kinds, routes and escalation.

-- config is the kind's settings; for webhooks the URL, every header value
-- and the signing secret are sealed with AAD channel:<org>:<id>.
CREATE TABLE notification_channels (
  id          uuid PRIMARY KEY,
  org_id      uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES organizations(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  kind        text NOT NULL CHECK (kind IN ('email', 'webhook')),
  name        text NOT NULL,
  config      jsonb NOT NULL,
  enabled     boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT true,
  created_by  uuid,
  created_at  timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  updated_at  timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  revision    bigint NOT NULL DEFAULT 1,
  UNIQUE (org_id, name),
  UNIQUE (org_id, id)
);

ALTER TABLE notification_channels ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
ALTER TABLE notification_channels FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
CREATE POLICY org_isolation ON notification_channels
  USING (org_id = NULLIF(current_setting('pulsix.org_id', true), '')::uuid);
CREATE POLICY org_isolation_bypass ON notification_channels
  USING (current_setting('pulsix.org_id', true) IS NULL OR current_setting('pulsix.org_id', true) = '');

-- One row per message and channel. (org_id, event_key, channel_id) is the
-- idempotency key: an event is enqueued at most once per channel however
-- often its source retries. payload is the rendered-from event (never log
-- output, variables or secrets). A pending row whose locked_until lies in
-- the future is being sent by a manager; attempts counts claims.
CREATE TABLE notification_deliveries (
  id              uuid PRIMARY KEY,
  org_id          uuid NOT NULL,
  channel_id      uuid NOT NULL,
  event_key       text NOT NULL,
  event_kind      text NOT NULL,
  payload         jsonb NOT NULL,
  status          text NOT NULL CONSTRAINT notification_deliveries_status_check
                    CHECK (status IN ('pending', 'sent', 'failed')),
  attempts        integer NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
  next_attempt_at timestamptz NOT NULL,
  locked_until    timestamptz,
  last_error      text NOT NULL DEFAULT '',
  created_at      timestamptz NOT NULL,
  sent_at         timestamptz,
  UNIQUE (org_id, event_key, channel_id),
  UNIQUE (org_id, id),
  FOREIGN KEY (org_id, channel_id) REFERENCES notification_channels (org_id, id) ON DELETE CASCADE
);
-- The dispatcher's due scan (bypass scope, every org) and the per-channel log.
CREATE INDEX notification_deliveries_due_idx ON notification_deliveries (next_attempt_at) WHERE status = 'pending';
CREATE INDEX notification_deliveries_channel_idx ON notification_deliveries (org_id, channel_id, created_at DESC);

ALTER TABLE notification_deliveries ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
ALTER TABLE notification_deliveries FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
CREATE POLICY org_isolation ON notification_deliveries
  USING (org_id = NULLIF(current_setting('pulsix.org_id', true), '')::uuid);
CREATE POLICY org_isolation_bypass ON notification_deliveries
  USING (current_setting('pulsix.org_id', true) IS NULL OR current_setting('pulsix.org_id', true) = '');

-- A schedule's notify_rules, merged per channel: one row per (schedule,
-- channel) with every event category that channel is told about, so a run
-- yields at most one message per channel by construction.
CREATE TABLE schedule_notifications (
  org_id      uuid NOT NULL,
  schedule_id uuid NOT NULL,
  channel_id  uuid NOT NULL,
  events      text[] NOT NULL CHECK (cardinality(events) > 0 AND events <@ ARRAY['failure', 'change', 'success', 'skipped']),
  PRIMARY KEY (org_id, schedule_id, channel_id),
  FOREIGN KEY (org_id, schedule_id) REFERENCES schedules (org_id, id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  FOREIGN KEY (org_id, channel_id) REFERENCES notification_channels (org_id, id) ON DELETE CASCADE
);

ALTER TABLE schedule_notifications ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
ALTER TABLE schedule_notifications FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
CREATE POLICY org_isolation ON schedule_notifications
  USING (org_id = NULLIF(current_setting('pulsix.org_id', true), '')::uuid);
CREATE POLICY org_isolation_bypass ON schedule_notifications
  USING (current_setting('pulsix.org_id', true) IS NULL OR current_setting('pulsix.org_id', true) = '');

-- The weekly digest, one row per organization. weekday is 0 (Sunday) to 6,
-- at_time HH:MM in timezone. next_run_at is NULL while disabled.
CREATE TABLE notification_digests (
  org_id      uuid PRIMARY KEY REFERENCES organizations(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  enabled     boolean NOT NULL,
  weekday     smallint NOT NULL CHECK (weekday BETWEEN 0 AND 6),
  at_time     text NOT NULL,
  timezone    text NOT NULL,
  next_run_at timestamptz,
  last_period text NOT NULL DEFAULT '',
  updated_at  timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  CHECK (enabled = (next_run_at IS NOT NULL))
);
CREATE INDEX notification_digests_due_idx ON notification_digests (next_run_at) WHERE enabled;

ALTER TABLE notification_digests ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
ALTER TABLE notification_digests FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
CREATE POLICY org_isolation ON notification_digests
  USING (org_id = NULLIF(current_setting('pulsix.org_id', true), '')::uuid);
CREATE POLICY org_isolation_bypass ON notification_digests
  USING (current_setting('pulsix.org_id', true) IS NULL OR current_setting('pulsix.org_id', true) = '');

CREATE TABLE notification_digest_channels (
  org_id     uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES notification_digests (org_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  channel_id uuid NOT NULL,
  PRIMARY KEY (org_id, channel_id),
  FOREIGN KEY (org_id, channel_id) REFERENCES notification_channels (org_id, id) ON DELETE CASCADE
);

ALTER TABLE notification_digest_channels ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
ALTER TABLE notification_digest_channels FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
CREATE POLICY org_isolation ON notification_digest_channels
  USING (org_id = NULLIF(current_setting('pulsix.org_id', true), '')::uuid);
CREATE POLICY org_isolation_bypass ON notification_digest_channels
  USING (current_setting('pulsix.org_id', true) IS NULL OR current_setting('pulsix.org_id', true) = '');

-- +goose Down
DROP TABLE notification_digest_channels;
DROP TABLE notification_digests;
DROP TABLE schedule_notifications;
DROP TABLE notification_deliveries;
DROP TABLE notification_channels;
```

- [ ] **Step 2: The channel queries and the generated code**

`internal/db/queries/notifications.sql` (Tasks 3, 9 and 10 append to it):

```sql
-- name: CreateNotificationChannel :one
INSERT INTO notification_channels (id, org_id, kind, name, config, enabled, created_by)
VALUES (@id, @org_id, @kind, @name, @config, @enabled, sqlc.narg(created_by))
RETURNING *;

-- name: GetNotificationChannel :one
SELECT * FROM notification_channels WHERE org_id = @org_id AND id = @id;

-- name: ListNotificationChannels :many
SELECT * FROM notification_channels WHERE org_id = @org_id ORDER BY name;

-- name: UpdateNotificationChannel :one
-- No row back means a stale revision or an unknown channel.
UPDATE notification_channels SET name = @name, config = @config, enabled = @enabled,
  revision = revision + 1, updated_at = now()
WHERE org_id = @org_id AND id = @id AND revision = @revision
RETURNING *;

-- name: DeleteNotificationChannel :execrows
DELETE FROM notification_channels WHERE org_id = @org_id AND id = @id;
```

`internal/notify/validate_test.go`:

```go
package notify

import (
	"errors"
	"strings"
	"testing"
)

func TestNormalizeEmail(t *testing.T) {
	got, err := normalizeEmail(&EmailConfig{Recipients: []string{" ops@example.test ", "dba@example.test", "OPS@example.test"}})
	if err != nil || len(got.Recipients) != 2 || got.Recipients[0] != "ops@example.test" {
		t.Fatalf("normalizeEmail = %+v, %v", got, err)
	}
	bad := []*EmailConfig{
		nil,
		{},
		{Recipients: []string{"not an address"}},
		{Recipients: []string{"Ops <ops@example.test>"}},
		{Recipients: []string{"ops@example.test\r\nBcc: x@example.test"}},
		{Recipients: strings.Split(strings.Repeat("a@example.test,", 21), ",")[:21]},
	}
	for _, in := range bad {
		if _, err := normalizeEmail(in); !errors.Is(err, ErrInvalidInput) {
			t.Errorf("normalizeEmail(%+v) = %v, want ErrInvalidInput", in, err)
		}
	}
}

func TestCheckURL(t *testing.T) {
	cases := []struct {
		raw, display string
	}{
		{"https://hooks.slack.com/services/T0/B0/secret", "https://hooks.slack.com/…"},
		{"http://ops.internal:8080", "http://ops.internal:8080"},
		{"https://ops.example.test/", "https://ops.example.test"},
		{"https://ops.example.test/?token=x", "https://ops.example.test/…"},
		{"https://[2001:db8::1]:8443/hook", "https://[2001:db8::1]:8443/…"},
	}
	for _, c := range cases {
		got, err := checkURL(c.raw)
		if err != nil || got != c.display {
			t.Errorf("checkURL(%q) = %q, %v; want %q", c.raw, got, err, c.display)
		}
	}
	for _, bad := range []string{"", "ftp://x.test/", "https://", "https://user:pw@x.test/", "https://x.test/#frag", "://x", "https://x.test/" + strings.Repeat("a", 2048)} {
		if _, err := checkURL(bad); !errors.Is(err, ErrInvalidInput) {
			t.Errorf("checkURL(%q) = %v, want ErrInvalidInput", bad, err)
		}
	}
}

func TestNormalizeMethod(t *testing.T) {
	for in, want := range map[string]string{"": "POST", "post": "POST", "PUT": "PUT"} {
		if got, err := normalizeMethod(in); err != nil || got != want {
			t.Errorf("normalizeMethod(%q) = %q, %v", in, got, err)
		}
	}
	if _, err := normalizeMethod("GET"); !errors.Is(err, ErrInvalidInput) {
		t.Fatalf("GET = %v", err)
	}
}

func TestCheckHeader(t *testing.T) {
	v := "Bearer abc"
	if err := checkHeader("Authorization", &v); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if err := checkHeader("X-Team", nil); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	long := strings.Repeat("a", maxHeaderValue+1)
	crlf := "a\r\nX-Evil: 1"
	cases := []struct {
		name  string
		value *string
	}{
		{"Bad Name", nil}, {"Host", nil}, {"content-length", nil}, {"X-Pulsix-Signature-256", nil},
		{"X-Long", &long}, {"X-Split", &crlf},
	}
	for _, c := range cases {
		if err := checkHeader(c.name, c.value); !errors.Is(err, ErrInvalidInput) {
			t.Errorf("checkHeader(%q) = %v, want ErrInvalidInput", c.name, err)
		}
	}
}

func TestNormalizeNameAndSecret(t *testing.T) {
	if n, err := normalizeName("  ops mail "); err != nil || n != "ops mail" {
		t.Fatalf("normalizeName = %q, %v", n, err)
	}
	for _, bad := range []string{"", "   ", strings.Repeat("x", 101), "a\tb"} {
		if _, err := normalizeName(bad); !errors.Is(err, ErrInvalidInput) {
			t.Errorf("normalizeName(%q) = %v", bad, err)
		}
	}
	if err := checkSigningSecret("short"); !errors.Is(err, ErrInvalidInput) {
		t.Fatal("a short secret was accepted")
	}
	if err := checkSigningSecret(strings.Repeat("s", 32)); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
}
```

`internal/notify/render_test.go`:

```go
package notify

import (
	"encoding/json"
	"errors"
	"strings"
	"testing"
)

func TestRenderWebhookBodyDefaultIsTheEventJSON(t *testing.T) {
	ev := sampleEvent()
	body, err := renderWebhookBody("", ev)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	var back Event
	if err := json.Unmarshal(body, &back); err != nil || back.Run.HostsFailed != 1 || back.Schedule.Name != "Nightly OS patching" {
		t.Fatalf("default body = %s (%v)", body, err)
	}
}

func TestRenderWebhookBodyTemplateEscapesThroughJSON(t *testing.T) {
	ev := sampleEvent()
	ev.Schedule.Name = `quote " and newline` + "\n"
	body, err := renderWebhookBody(`{"text": {{json .Schedule.Name}}, "failed": {{.Run.HostsFailed}}}`, ev)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	var doc map[string]any
	if err := json.Unmarshal(body, &doc); err != nil || doc["text"] != ev.Schedule.Name || doc["failed"] != float64(1) {
		t.Fatalf("body %s is not the expected JSON (%v)", body, err)
	}
}

func TestRenderWebhookBodyLimits(t *testing.T) {
	if _, err := renderWebhookBody(`{{printf "%070000d" 1}}`, sampleEvent()); !errors.Is(err, errBodyTooLarge) {
		t.Fatalf("oversized body = %v", err)
	}
	if _, err := renderWebhookBody(`{{.Run.Nope}}`, sampleEvent()); err == nil {
		t.Fatal("an unknown field rendered")
	}
	if _, err := renderWebhookBody(`{{`, sampleEvent()); err == nil {
		t.Fatal("an unparsable template rendered")
	}
}

func TestCheckBodyTemplate(t *testing.T) {
	if err := checkBodyTemplate(""); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if err := checkBodyTemplate(`{"s": {{json .Schedule.Name}}}`); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	for _, bad := range []string{`{{.Schedule.Title}}`, `{{ if }}`, strings.Repeat("x", maxTemplateLength+1)} {
		if err := checkBodyTemplate(bad); !errors.Is(err, ErrInvalidInput) {
			t.Errorf("checkBodyTemplate(%.20q) = %v", bad, err)
		}
	}
}

func TestRenderEmail(t *testing.T) {
	ev := sampleEvent()
	subject, body := renderEmail(ev)
	if subject != "[Pulsix] Nightly OS patching: failed" || !strings.Contains(body, "Hosts:     12 (ok 11, changed 9, failed 1)") || !strings.Contains(body, ev.URL) {
		t.Fatalf("run email = %q\n%s", subject, body)
	}
	ev.Run.Result = "succeeded"
	if subject, _ = renderEmail(ev); subject != "[Pulsix] Nightly OS patching: succeeded with changes" {
		t.Fatalf("succeeded with changes = %q", subject)
	}
	ev.Kind = EventScheduleSkipped
	if subject, body = renderEmail(ev); subject != "[Pulsix] Nightly OS patching: skipped" || !strings.Contains(body, "skipped_maintenance") {
		t.Fatalf("skipped email = %q\n%s", subject, body)
	}
	ev.Kind = EventScheduleFailed
	if subject, _ = renderEmail(ev); subject != "[Pulsix] Nightly OS patching: could not start" {
		t.Fatalf("failed decision email = %q", subject)
	}
	ev.Kind = EventDigest
	if subject, body = renderEmail(ev); subject != "[Pulsix] Weekly schedule digest for Example" || !strings.Contains(body, "Nightly OS patching: 2 run(s), 1 failure(s), 1 skipped or missed") || !strings.Contains(body, "Slowest runs:") {
		t.Fatalf("digest email = %q\n%s", subject, body)
	}
	ev.Digest.Schedules, ev.Digest.Slowest = nil, nil
	if _, body = renderEmail(ev); !strings.Contains(body, "No schedules.") {
		t.Fatalf("empty digest = %s", body)
	}
	ev.Kind = EventTest
	if subject, _ = renderEmail(ev); subject != "[Pulsix] Test notification from Example" {
		t.Fatalf("test email = %q", subject)
	}
}

func TestEventCategories(t *testing.T) {
	if got := runCategories("failed", 3); len(got) != 2 || got[0] != CategoryFailure || got[1] != CategoryChange {
		t.Fatalf("failed with changes = %v", got)
	}
	if got := runCategories("cancelled", 0); len(got) != 1 || got[0] != CategoryFailure {
		t.Fatalf("cancelled = %v", got)
	}
	if got := runCategories("succeeded", 0); len(got) != 1 || got[0] != CategorySuccess {
		t.Fatalf("succeeded = %v", got)
	}
	for outcome, want := range map[string]EventKind{"skipped_overlap": EventScheduleSkipped, "skipped_maintenance": EventScheduleSkipped, "missed": EventScheduleSkipped, "failed": EventScheduleFailed} {
		if _, kind, ok := decisionCategory(outcome); !ok || kind != want {
			t.Errorf("decisionCategory(%s) = %s, %v", outcome, kind, ok)
		}
	}
	if _, _, ok := decisionCategory("triggered"); ok {
		t.Fatal("a triggered decision is reported when its run finishes, not now")
	}
	if c, ok := ParseCategory("change"); !ok || c != CategoryChange {
		t.Fatal("ParseCategory(change)")
	}
	if _, ok := ParseCategory("always"); ok {
		t.Fatal("ParseCategory accepted an unknown category")
	}
}
```
