```tsx
});

describe('calendar helpers', () => {
  it('computes weeks, ranges and tones', () => {
    const wed = new Date(2026, 8, 23, 15, 30);
    expect(startOfWeek(wed)).toEqual(new Date(2026, 8, 21));
    const { from, to } = rangeFor('month', new Date(2026, 8, 21));
    expect(from).toEqual(new Date(2026, 7, 31));
    expect(addDays(from, 42)).toEqual(to);
    expect(shift('week', new Date(2026, 8, 21), 1)).toEqual(new Date(2026, 8, 28));
    expect(shift('month', new Date(2026, 8, 21), 1)).toEqual(new Date(2026, 9, 5));
    expect(shift('month', new Date(2026, 8, 21), -1)).toEqual(new Date(2026, 7, 3));
    // The week of 31 Aug 2026 belongs to September (its Thursday is 3 Sep).
    expect(monthOf(new Date(2026, 7, 31))).toEqual(new Date(2026, 8, 1));
    expect(entryTone(entry({}))).toBe('unknown');
    expect(entryTone(entry({ state: 'triggered', job_status: 'running' }))).toBe('accent');
    expect(entryTone(entry({ state: 'triggered', job_status: 'cancelled' }))).toBe('warn');
    expect(entryTone(entry({ state: 'failed' }))).toBe('crit');
    expect(entryTone(entry({ state: 'missed' }))).toBe('warn');
    const cells = weekCells([entry({ at: new Date(2026, 8, 22, 4).toISOString() }), entry({ at: new Date(2026, 8, 29).toISOString() })], new Date(2026, 8, 21));
    expect(cells[1][1]).toHaveLength(1);
    expect(cells.flat(2)).toHaveLength(1);
    expect(windowsIn([{ window_id: 'w', name: 'x', target_kind: 'org', start: new Date(2026, 8, 22, 2).toISOString(), end: new Date(2026, 8, 22, 4).toISOString() }], new Date(2026, 8, 22, 3), new Date(2026, 8, 22, 6))).toHaveLength(1);
  });
});
```

```ts
import { useQuery } from '@tanstack/react-query';
import { api, unwrap } from '../client.ts';

/** useCalendar loads the schedule occurrences and maintenance windows of
 * [from, to) (ISO 8601, at most six weeks apart). */
export function useCalendar(org: string, from: string, to: string) {
  return useQuery({
    queryKey: ['calendar', org, from, to],
    queryFn: () => unwrap(api.GET('/api/v1/orgs/{org}/calendar', { params: { path: { org }, query: { from, to } } })),
    enabled: !!org,
  });
}
```

`web/src/features/calendar/calendar.ts`:

```ts
import type { Tone } from '../../ui/badge.tsx';
import type { CalendarEntry, CalendarWindow } from '../../api/types.ts';

/** MAINTENANCE_COLOR draws maintenance windows (the mockup's violet — not
 * a state colour, so it cannot be mistaken for one). */
export const MAINTENANCE_COLOR = '#A78BFA';

export type CalendarView = 'week' | 'month' | 'list';

const DAY = 86_400_000;

/** startOfWeek is Monday 00:00 (local time) of d's week. */
export function startOfWeek(d: Date): Date {
  const out = new Date(d.getFullYear(), d.getMonth(), d.getDate());
  const shift = (out.getDay() + 6) % 7; // Monday = 0
  out.setDate(out.getDate() - shift);
  return out;
}

export function addDays(d: Date, n: number): Date {
  const out = new Date(d);
  out.setDate(out.getDate() + n);
  return out;
}

/** monthOf is the month an anchor week belongs to: the month of its
 * Thursday (the ISO week rule), so a week starting on 31 August belongs to
 * September. */
export function monthOf(anchor: Date): Date {
  const mid = addDays(anchor, 3);
  return new Date(mid.getFullYear(), mid.getMonth(), 1);
}

/** rangeFor is what a view shows around anchor (a Monday): a week, or the
 * six weeks from the Monday on or before the first of the anchor's month
 * (the API's maximum range). */
export function rangeFor(view: CalendarView, anchor: Date): { from: Date; to: Date } {
  if (view === 'month') {
    const from = startOfWeek(monthOf(anchor));
    return { from, to: addDays(from, 42) };
  }
  return { from: anchor, to: addDays(anchor, 7) };
}

/** shift moves the anchor one view back or forth; in the month view to
 * the week of the 8th of the next or previous month, whose Thursday always
 * lies in that month. */
export function shift(view: CalendarView, anchor: Date, dir: 1 | -1): Date {
  if (view === 'month') {
    const m = monthOf(anchor);
    return startOfWeek(new Date(m.getFullYear(), m.getMonth() + dir, 8));
  }
  return addDays(anchor, 7 * dir);
}

/** entryTone colours an occurrence: planned muted, a run by its status,
 * skipped and missed as warnings, a failed start as critical. */
export function entryTone(e: CalendarEntry): Tone {
  switch (e.state) {
    case 'planned':
      return 'unknown';
    case 'triggered':
      if (e.job_status === 'succeeded') return 'ok';
      if (e.job_status === 'failed') return 'crit';
      if (e.job_status === 'cancelled') return 'warn';
      return 'accent';
    case 'failed':
      return 'crit';
    default:
      return 'warn';
  }
}

export const BANDS = [0, 3, 6, 9, 12, 15, 18, 21];

export interface CellItem {
  scheduleId: string;
  name: string;
  count: number;
  /** The last occurrence in the cell, what a click opens. */
  entry: CalendarEntry;
}

/** weekCells buckets entries into 7 days × 8 three-hour bands (local
 * time), one item per schedule and cell with a count. */
export function weekCells(entries: CalendarEntry[], start: Date): CellItem[][][] {
  const cells: CellItem[][][] = Array.from({ length: 7 }, () => BANDS.map(() => []));
  for (const e of entries) {
    const at = new Date(e.at);
    const day = Math.floor((new Date(at.getFullYear(), at.getMonth(), at.getDate()).getTime() - start.getTime()) / DAY);
    if (day < 0 || day > 6) continue;
    const band = Math.floor(at.getHours() / 3);
    const cell = cells[day][band];
    const item = cell.find((c) => c.scheduleId === e.schedule_id);
    if (item) {
      item.count++;
      item.entry = e;
    } else {
      cell.push({ scheduleId: e.schedule_id, name: e.schedule_name, count: 1, entry: e });
    }
  }
  return cells;
}

/** windowsIn lists the windows overlapping [from, to). */
export function windowsIn(windows: CalendarWindow[], from: Date, to: Date): CalendarWindow[] {
  return windows.filter((w) => new Date(w.start) < to && new Date(w.end) > from);
}

/** dayEntries groups entries by local day (yyyy-mm-dd). */
export function dayKey(d: Date): string {
  return `${d.getFullYear()}-${String(d.getMonth() + 1).padStart(2, '0')}-${String(d.getDate()).padStart(2, '0')}`;
}
```

`web/src/features/calendar/ScheduleCalendar.tsx`:

```tsx
import { useState } from 'react';
import { useNavigate } from '@tanstack/react-router';
import { ChevronLeft, ChevronRight } from 'lucide-react';
import { Button } from '../../ui/button.tsx';
import { Pill } from '../../ui/badge.tsx';
import { useCalendar } from '../../api/hooks/useCalendar.ts';
import type { CalendarEntry, CalendarWindow } from '../../api/types.ts';
import { BANDS, MAINTENANCE_COLOR, type CalendarView, addDays, dayKey, entryTone, monthOf, rangeFor, shift, startOfWeek, weekCells, windowsIn } from './calendar.ts';

const TONE_VAR = { ok: 'var(--px-ok)', warn: 'var(--px-warn)', crit: 'var(--px-crit)', unknown: 'var(--px-unknown)', accent: 'var(--px-accent)' } as const;
const dayLabel = (d: Date) => d.toLocaleDateString(undefined, { weekday: 'short', day: 'numeric' });

/** ScheduleCalendar is the Scheduler artboard's calendar (spec 2026-09-18
 * §8, mockup "Week · 8–14 Sep"): scheduled runs — planned ones muted, past
 * ones coloured by their result — and maintenance windows as violet bars,
 * as a week grid, a month grid or a list. Clicking an occurrence opens its
 * run, or the schedule when no run exists. */
export function ScheduleCalendar({ org }: { org: string }) {
  const navigate = useNavigate();
  const [view, setView] = useState<CalendarView>('week');
  const [anchor, setAnchor] = useState(() => startOfWeek(new Date()));
  const { from, to } = rangeFor(view, anchor);
  const cal = useCalendar(org, from.toISOString(), to.toISOString());
  const entries = cal.data?.entries ?? [];
  const windows = cal.data?.maintenance ?? [];

  function open(e: CalendarEntry) {
    if (e.job_id) void navigate({ to: '/o/$org/runs/$id', params: { org, id: e.job_id } });
    else void navigate({ to: '/o/$org/schedules', params: { org }, search: { id: e.schedule_id, template: undefined } });
  }

  const title =
    view === 'month'
      ? monthOf(anchor).toLocaleDateString(undefined, { month: 'long', year: 'numeric' })
      : `Week · ${from.toLocaleDateString(undefined, { day: 'numeric', month: 'short' })} – ${addDays(to, -1).toLocaleDateString(undefined, { day: 'numeric', month: 'short' })}`;

  return (
    <section aria-label="Calendar" className="flex flex-col overflow-hidden rounded-[10px] border border-border bg-card">
      <div className="flex items-center gap-2 border-b border-border px-4 py-2.5">
        <Button type="button" variant="ghost" aria-label="Previous" onClick={() => setAnchor(shift(view, anchor, -1))}>
          <ChevronLeft size={14} />
        </Button>
        <h2 className="text-[13px] font-semibold">{title}</h2>
        <Button type="button" variant="ghost" aria-label="Next" onClick={() => setAnchor(shift(view, anchor, 1))}>
          <ChevronRight size={14} />
        </Button>
        <div role="tablist" className="ml-auto flex gap-1">
          {(['week', 'month', 'list'] as const).map((v) => (
            <button
              key={v}
              type="button"
              role="tab"
              aria-selected={view === v}
              className={`rounded-md border px-2.5 py-1 text-xs capitalize ${view === v ? 'border-primary text-primary' : 'border-border text-muted-foreground'}`}
              onClick={() => setView(v)}
            >
              {v}
            </button>
          ))}
        </div>
        <span className="flex items-center gap-1.5 text-[11px] text-muted-foreground">
          <span className="h-1.5 w-1.5 rounded-full" style={{ backgroundColor: MAINTENANCE_COLOR }} /> maintenance
        </span>
      </div>
      {cal.data?.truncated && <p className="px-4 pt-2 text-[11px] text-muted-foreground">Some very frequent schedules are shown in part.</p>}
      {view === 'week' && <WeekGrid start={from} entries={entries} windows={windows} onOpen={open} />}
      {view === 'month' && <MonthGrid start={from} month={monthOf(anchor).getMonth()} entries={entries} windows={windows} onOpen={open} />}
      {view === 'list' && <ListView entries={entries} windows={windows} onOpen={open} />}
    </section>
  );
}

function Chip({ label, color, onClick }: { label: string; color: string; onClick?: () => void }) {
  return (
    <button
      type="button"
      onClick={onClick}
      className="truncate rounded-[3px] px-1 text-left text-[9px]"
      style={{ color, backgroundColor: `color-mix(in srgb, ${color} 13%, transparent)` }}
    >
      {label}
    </button>
  );
}

function WindowBar({ w }: { w: CalendarWindow }) {
  return <Chip label={`Maintenance · ${w.name}`} color={MAINTENANCE_COLOR} />;
}

function WeekGrid({ start, entries, windows, onOpen }: { start: Date; entries: CalendarEntry[]; windows: CalendarWindow[]; onOpen: (e: CalendarEntry) => void }) {
  const cells = weekCells(entries, start);
  const days = Array.from({ length: 7 }, (_, i) => addDays(start, i));
  const today = dayKey(new Date());
  return (
    <div className="grid gap-1 px-4 py-3 text-[10px] text-muted-foreground" style={{ gridTemplateColumns: '44px repeat(7, minmax(0, 1fr))' }}>
      <span />
      {days.map((d) => (
        <span key={d.toISOString()} className={`text-center font-semibold ${dayKey(d) === today ? 'text-primary' : ''}`}>
          {dayLabel(d)}
        </span>
      ))}
      {BANDS.map((h, band) => (
        <WeekRow key={h} hour={h} band={band} days={days} cells={cells} windows={windows} onOpen={onOpen} />
      ))}
    </div>
  );
}

function WeekRow({ hour, band, days, cells, windows, onOpen }: {
  hour: number; band: number; days: Date[]; cells: ReturnType<typeof weekCells>; windows: CalendarWindow[]; onOpen: (e: CalendarEntry) => void;
}) {
  return (
    <>
      <span className="font-mono">{String(hour).padStart(2, '0')}:00</span>
      {days.map((d, day) => {
        const from = new Date(d.getFullYear(), d.getMonth(), d.getDate(), hour);
        const to = new Date(d.getFullYear(), d.getMonth(), d.getDate(), hour + 3);
        return (
          <div key={day} data-cell={`${day}-${band}`} className="flex min-h-[26px] flex-col gap-0.5 rounded border border-border bg-background p-0.5">
            {cells[day][band].map((item) => (
              <Chip
                key={item.scheduleId}
                label={item.count > 1 ? `${item.name} ×${item.count}` : item.name}
                color={TONE_VAR[entryTone(item.entry)]}
                onClick={() => onOpen(item.entry)}
              />
            ))}
            {windowsIn(windows, from, to).map((w) => (
              <WindowBar key={w.window_id + w.start} w={w} />
            ))}
          </div>
        );
      })}
    </>
  );
}

function MonthGrid({ start, month, entries, windows, onOpen }: {
  start: Date; month: number; entries: CalendarEntry[]; windows: CalendarWindow[]; onOpen: (e: CalendarEntry) => void;
}) {
  const days = Array.from({ length: 42 }, (_, i) => addDays(start, i));
  const byDay = new Map<string, CalendarEntry[]>();
  for (const e of entries) {
    const k = dayKey(new Date(e.at));
    byDay.set(k, [...(byDay.get(k) ?? []), e]);
  }
  return (
    <div className="grid grid-cols-7 gap-1 px-4 py-3 text-[10px]">
      {days.map((d) => {
        const list = byDay.get(dayKey(d)) ?? [];
        const names = [...new Map(list.map((e) => [e.schedule_id, e])).values()];
        const inWindow = windowsIn(windows, d, addDays(d, 1)).length > 0;
        return (
          <div key={d.toISOString()} data-day={dayKey(d)} className={`flex min-h-[64px] flex-col gap-0.5 rounded border border-border p-1 ${d.getMonth() === month ? 'bg-background' : 'opacity-50'}`}>
            <span className="flex items-center justify-between text-muted-foreground">
              {d.getDate()}
              {inWindow && <span aria-label="maintenance" className="h-1.5 w-1.5 rounded-full" style={{ backgroundColor: MAINTENANCE_COLOR }} />}
            </span>
            {names.slice(0, 3).map((e) => (
              <Chip key={e.schedule_id} label={e.schedule_name} color={TONE_VAR[entryTone(e)]} onClick={() => onOpen(e)} />
            ))}
            {names.length > 3 && <span className="text-muted-foreground">+{names.length - 3} more</span>}
          </div>
        );
      })}
    </div>
  );
}

function ListView({ entries, windows, onOpen }: { entries: CalendarEntry[]; windows: CalendarWindow[]; onOpen: (e: CalendarEntry) => void }) {
  type Row = { at: Date; entry?: CalendarEntry; window?: CalendarWindow };
  const rows: Row[] = [...entries.map((e) => ({ at: new Date(e.at), entry: e })), ...windows.map((w) => ({ at: new Date(w.start), window: w }))].sort(
    (a, b) => a.at.getTime() - b.at.getTime(),
  );
  if (rows.length === 0) return <p className="px-4 py-6 text-center text-xs text-muted-foreground">Nothing scheduled in this range.</p>;
  return (
    <ul className="flex flex-col divide-y divide-border">
      {rows.map((r, i) => (
        <li key={i} className="grid grid-cols-[150px_minmax(0,1fr)_150px] items-center gap-3 px-4 py-2 text-xs" data-list-row>
          <span className="font-mono text-muted-foreground">{r.at.toLocaleString(undefined, { dateStyle: 'short', timeStyle: 'short' })}</span>
          {r.entry ? (
            <button type="button" className="truncate text-left hover:text-primary" onClick={() => onOpen(r.entry!)}>
              {r.entry.schedule_name}
            </button>
          ) : (
            <span className="truncate" style={{ color: MAINTENANCE_COLOR }}>
              Maintenance · {r.window!.name} (until {new Date(r.window!.end).toLocaleString(undefined, { dateStyle: 'short', timeStyle: 'short' })})
            </span>
          )}
          <span>{r.entry && <Pill tone={entryTone(r.entry)}>{r.entry.state === 'triggered' ? r.entry.job_status ?? 'triggered' : r.entry.state}</Pill>}</span>
        </li>
      ))}
    </ul>
  );
}
```

```tsx
import { useState } from 'react';
import { useNavigate, useParams, useSearch } from '@tanstack/react-router';
import { Link } from '@tanstack/react-router';
import { Terminal } from 'lucide-react';
import { Card, GridHeader, GridRow } from '../../ui/table.tsx';
import { Pill } from '../../ui/badge.tsx';
import { Button } from '../../ui/button.tsx';
import { LoadMoreButton } from '../../ui/LoadMoreButton.tsx';
import { QueryLoading } from '../../ui/QueryState.tsx';
import { useQueryGuard } from '../../ui/useQueryGuard.tsx';
import { useJobs } from '../../api/hooks/useJobs.ts';
import { useOrg } from '../../api/hooks/useOrgs.ts';
import { useSchedules } from '../../api/hooks/useSchedules.ts';
import { may } from '../../lib/roles.ts';
import { AdhocDialog } from './AdhocDialog.tsx';
import { kindLabel, shortId, statusTone } from './status.ts';
import { TRIGGER_LABEL, triggerLabel } from './trigger.ts';

const COLUMNS = '90px 120px 110px 90px minmax(0, 0.7fr) 90px 110px';

const STATUSES = ['queued', 'running', 'succeeded', 'failed', 'cancelled', 'error'];
const KINDS = ['run', 'connection_test', 'bootstrap', 'rotate_key', 'revoke_key', 'sync_repo'];

/**
 * RunsPage is the org's run list (spec §7, backlog 2.12): keyset-paginated,
 * filterable by status, kind, trigger and schedule (spec 2026-09-18 §8),
 * each row a link into the run view. The filters live in the URL
 * (?status=&kind=&trigger=&schedule=), validated by the route.
 */
export function RunsPage() {
  const { org } = useParams({ strict: false }) as { org: string };
  const navigate = useNavigate();
  const search = useSearch({ strict: false }) as { status?: string; kind?: string; trigger?: string; schedule?: string };
  const jobs = useJobs(org, { status: search.status, kind: search.kind, trigger: search.trigger, triggerRef: search.schedule });
  const schedules = useSchedules(search.trigger === 'schedule' ? org : '');
  const orgInfo = useOrg(org);
  const canRun = may(orgInfo.data?.org.role, 'jobs.run');
  const [adhocOpen, setAdhocOpen] = useState(false);

  const guard = useQueryGuard(jobs);
  if (guard) return guard;
  if (!jobs.data) return <QueryLoading />;
  const rows = jobs.data.pages.flatMap((p) => p.jobs ?? []);

  function filter(next: { status?: string; kind?: string; trigger?: string; schedule?: string }) {
    const trigger = next.trigger ?? search.trigger;
    void navigate({
      to: '/o/$org/runs',
      params: { org },
      search: {
        status: next.status ?? search.status,
        kind: next.kind ?? search.kind,
        trigger,
        // A schedule filter only makes sense for scheduled runs.
        schedule: trigger === 'schedule' ? (next.schedule ?? search.schedule) : undefined,
      },
    });
  }

  return (
    <>
      <div className="flex items-center justify-between">
        <div className="flex flex-col gap-2">
          <h1 className="text-xl font-semibold">Runs</h1>
          <span className="text-[13px] text-muted-foreground">{rows.length} runs · every job the workers executed for this organization</span>
        </div>
        <div className="flex gap-2">
          <select aria-label="Filter by status" className="h-[34px] rounded-md border border-border bg-card px-2 text-xs" value={search.status ?? ''} onChange={(e) => filter({ status: e.target.value })}>
            <option value="">All statuses</option>
            {STATUSES.map((s) => (
              <option key={s} value={s}>{s}</option>
            ))}
          </select>
          <select aria-label="Filter by kind" className="h-[34px] rounded-md border border-border bg-card px-2 text-xs" value={search.kind ?? ''} onChange={(e) => filter({ kind: e.target.value })}>
            <option value="">All kinds</option>
            {KINDS.map((k) => (
              <option key={k} value={k}>{kindLabel(k)}</option>
            ))}
          </select>
          <select aria-label="Filter by trigger" className="h-[34px] rounded-md border border-border bg-card px-2 text-xs" value={search.trigger ?? ''} onChange={(e) => filter({ trigger: e.target.value })}>
            <option value="">All triggers</option>
            {Object.entries(TRIGGER_LABEL).map(([k, label]) => (
              <option key={k} value={k}>{label}</option>
            ))}
          </select>
          {search.trigger === 'schedule' && (
            <select aria-label="Filter by schedule" className="h-[34px] rounded-md border border-border bg-card px-2 text-xs" value={search.schedule ?? ''} onChange={(e) => filter({ schedule: e.target.value })}>
              <option value="">All schedules</option>
              {(schedules.data?.schedules ?? []).map((sc) => (
                <option key={sc.id} value={sc.id}>{sc.name}</option>
              ))}
            </select>
          )}
          {canRun && (
            <Button variant="primary" onClick={() => setAdhocOpen(true)}>
              <Terminal size={15} /> Run command
            </Button>
          )}
        </div>
      </div>
      <Card>
        <GridHeader columns={COLUMNS}>
          <span>Run</span>
          <span>Kind</span>
          <span>Status</span>
          <span>Trigger</span>
          <span>Template</span>
          <span>Zone</span>
          <span>Started</span>
        </GridHeader>
        {rows.map((j) => (
          <GridRow key={j.id} columns={COLUMNS}>
            <Link to="/o/$org/runs/$id" params={{ org, id: j.id }} className="font-mono text-[13px] hover:text-primary">
              {shortId(j.id)}
            </Link>
            <span className="text-xs">{kindLabel(j.kind)}</span>
            <span><Pill tone={statusTone(j.status)}>{j.status}</Pill></span>
            <span className="text-xs">{triggerLabel(j.trigger)}</span>
            <span className="truncate font-mono text-xs text-muted-foreground">{j.template_id ? shortId(j.template_id) : '—'}</span>
            <span className="font-mono text-xs text-muted-foreground">{shortId(j.zone_id)}</span>
            <span className="text-xs text-muted-foreground">{j.created_at.slice(0, 16).replace('T', ' ')}</span>
          </GridRow>
        ))}
        {rows.length === 0 && <p className="px-4 py-6 text-center text-xs text-muted-foreground">No runs match.</p>}
      </Card>
      {jobs.hasNextPage && <LoadMoreButton onClick={() => void jobs.fetchNextPage()} loading={jobs.isFetchingNextPage} />}
      {adhocOpen && <AdhocDialog org={org} onClose={() => setAdhocOpen(false)} />}
    </>
  );
}
```

```markdown
## Calendar

Below the list of schedules, the calendar shows what ran and what will run:

- **Week** — seven days in three-hour rows, the way a wall planner shows them. A
  schedule that fires several times in one block appears once with a count.
- **Month** — six weeks at a glance, up to three schedules per day.
- **List** — every occurrence and maintenance window in order.

Upcoming runs are grey. Past runs are coloured by their result: green for succeeded,
red for failed or not started, yellow for skipped, missed or cancelled, cyan while
still running. Maintenance windows are violet bars. Click a past run to open it, or
an upcoming one to open its schedule. A schedule that fires very often (every
minute, say) is shown only in part, and the calendar says so. Times are shown in
your browser's time zone.
```

In `reference/api.md`, append to the "Schedules" section:

```markdown
`GET /api/v1/orgs/{slug}/calendar?from=…&to=…` (RFC 3339 times, at most six weeks
apart) returns `{entries, maintenance, truncated}`. Each entry is one occurrence of a
schedule: `state` is `planned` for one still ahead, otherwise the recorded outcome
(`triggered`, `skipped_overlap`, `skipped_maintenance`, `missed`, `failed`); a
triggered one carries `job_id` (its run) and `job_status`. `maintenance` lists every
window's stretches in the range. `truncated` is true when a very frequent schedule's
occurrences were cut.
```

```markdown
The runs list `GET /api/v1/orgs/{slug}/jobs` also filters by `trigger` (`manual`,
`schedule`, `webhook`, `system`) and by `trigger_ref`, the id of the schedule or
webhook trigger that started the runs.
```

### Task 12: Milestone E2E (a failing scheduled run notifies once, `exclude_hosts` leaves a host out, the calendar)

This is the v0.2d gate, run against the fully wired manager, a real worker loop, the SSH fixtures and the database container, like the v0.2b and v0.2c gates. It covers three things:
1. A schedule fires on the injected clock, its run fails, and a signed webhook channel on a loopback receiver (admitted through `PULSIX_NOTIFY_ALLOW_CIDRS`) receives exactly one message. That message carries a valid signature and the run's id, and holds no run output or variables. The delivery log says `sent`.
2. A maintenance window on a second host makes an `exclude_hosts` schedule run on the first host only.
3. The calendar returns the failed past run, the guarded schedule's planned runs and the window.

**Files:**
- Create: `internal/e2e/notifications_test.go`
- Modify: `internal/e2e/ansible_test.go` (`wiring.notifyAllow` → `config.Config.NotifyAllowCIDRs`)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `wireScenario`, `wiring`, `fakeClock`, `app.WithScheduleClock`, `scheduleOut`, `jobListOut`, `templateOut`, `hostOut`, `pinHostKey`, `awaitScheduleRun`, `awaitJob`, `createEditorPlaybook`, `createTemplate`, `failTemplate`, `greetTemplate` (all existing in `internal/e2e`). Every API from Tasks 4–11.

- [ ] **Step 1: The wiring option**

```go

	// gate admits its loopback webhook receiver.

```

and to the `config.Config` literal in `wireScenario`: `NotifyAllowCIDRs: w.notifyAllow,`.

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the E2E**

`internal/e2e/notifications_test.go`:

```go
// The v0.2d milestone gate: a scheduled run fails and a signed webhook
// channel receives exactly one message for it, a maintenance window with
// exclude_hosts leaves a host out of a scheduled run, and the calendar
// shows the schedules' occurrences together with the window.
package e2e

import (
	"context"
	"crypto/hmac"
	"crypto/sha256"
	"encoding/hex"
	"encoding/json"
	"io"
	"net/http"
	"net/http/httptest"
	"net/netip"
	"net/url"
	"os/exec"
	"strings"
	"sync"
	"testing"
	"time"

	"github.com/noixdev/pulsix/internal/app"
)

// notifySecret signs the webhook channel's requests.
const notifySecret = "e2e-notify-signing-secret"

// received is one request the webhook receiver got.
type received struct {
	event, delivery, timestamp, signature string
	body                                  []byte
}

// hookReceiver is the webhook channel's endpoint: it records every request
// and answers 204.
type hookReceiver struct {
	mu   sync.Mutex
	reqs []received
}

func (h *hookReceiver) ServeHTTP(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
	body, _ := io.ReadAll(r.Body)
	h.mu.Lock()
	h.reqs = append(h.reqs, received{
		event: r.Header.Get("X-Pulsix-Event"), delivery: r.Header.Get("X-Pulsix-Delivery"),
		timestamp: r.Header.Get("X-Pulsix-Timestamp"), signature: r.Header.Get("X-Pulsix-Signature-256"), body: body,
	})
	h.mu.Unlock()
	w.WriteHeader(http.StatusNoContent)
}

func (h *hookReceiver) snapshot() []received {
	h.mu.Lock()
	defer h.mu.Unlock()
	return append([]received(nil), h.reqs...)
}

func TestNotificationsEndToEnd(t *testing.T) {
	if _, err := exec.LookPath("docker"); err != nil {
		t.Skipf("no docker for the testcontainer database: %v", err)
	}
	ctx, cancel := context.WithCancel(context.Background())
	t.Cleanup(cancel)
	rec := &hookReceiver{}
	hook := httptest.NewServer(rec)
	t.Cleanup(hook.Close)
	clock := &fakeClock{}
	s := wireScenario(t, ctx, cancel, wiring{
		scheduleTick: 100 * time.Millisecond,
		opts:         []app.Option{app.WithScheduleClock(clock.Now)},
		notifyAllow:  []netip.Prefix{netip.MustParsePrefix("127.0.0.0/8")},
	})
	s.notifications(clock, rec, hook.URL)
}

type (
	channelCreatedOut struct {
		Channel struct {
			ID string `json:"id"`
		} `json:"channel"`
	}
	calendarOut struct {
		Entries []struct {
			ScheduleID string `json:"schedule_id"`
			State      string `json:"state"`
			JobStatus  string `json:"job_status"`
		} `json:"entries"`
		Maintenance []struct {
			Name string `json:"name"`
		} `json:"maintenance"`
	}
)

func (s *scenario) notifications(clock *fakeClock, rec *hookReceiver, hookURL string) {
	s.t.Helper()
	prefix := "/api/v1/" + s.apiPath()
	s.pinHostKey()

	// --- A signed webhook channel and a failing schedule notifying it -------
	var ch channelCreatedOut
	s.post(prefix+"/notification-channels", map[string]any{
		"kind": "webhook", "name": "e2e receiver",
		"webhook": map[string]any{"url": hookURL + "/pulsix?token=e2e", "signed": true, "signing_secret": notifySecret},
	}, &ch)
	s.failPB = s.createEditorPlaybook("fail", failTemplate(s.t))
	s.failTID = s.createTemplate("deliberate", s.failPB, map[string]any{"should_fail": "true"})
	var failing scheduleOut
	s.post(prefix+"/schedules", map[string]any{
		"template_id": s.failTID, "name": "failing nightly", "mode": "cron", "cron": "*/5 * * * *", "timezone": "UTC",
		"guards": map[string]any{"catch_up": map[string]any{"enabled": true, "window": "1h"}},
	}, &failing)
	// failure and change both name the channel: still one message per run.
	s.put(prefix+"/schedules/"+failing.Schedule.ID+"/notifications", map[string]any{"rules": []map[string]any{
		{"on": "failure", "channels": []string{ch.Channel.ID}},
		{"on": "change", "channels": []string{ch.Channel.ID}},
	}}, &map[string]any{})

	clock.setAt(*failing.Schedule.NextRunAt)
	fired := s.awaitScheduleRun(failing.Schedule.ID, 30*time.Second)
	if fired.Outcome != "triggered" || fired.JobGroupID == "" {
		s.t.Fatalf("failing schedule run = %+v", fired)
	}
	var group jobListOut
	s.get(prefix+"/jobs?group="+fired.JobGroupID, &group)
	if job := s.awaitJob(group.Jobs[0].ID, "run", 90*time.Second); job.Job.Status != "failed" {
		s.t.Fatalf("scheduled run = %q (error %q), want failed%s", job.Job.Status, job.Job.Error, s.runnerOutput())
	}
	// Stop the schedule, so moving the clock later fires nothing more.
	s.post(prefix+"/schedules/"+failing.Schedule.ID+"/pause", struct{}{}, &map[string]any{})

	// --- Exactly one message, signed, naming the failed run ------------------
	deadline := time.Now().Add(30 * time.Second)
	for len(rec.snapshot()) == 0 {
		if time.Now().After(deadline) {
			s.t.Fatal("the webhook channel received nothing in 30s")
		}
		time.Sleep(100 * time.Millisecond)
	}
	time.Sleep(5 * time.Second) // two dispatcher intervals and more: a duplicate would have arrived
	got := rec.snapshot()
	if len(got) != 1 {
		s.t.Fatalf("the channel received %d messages, want exactly 1", len(got))
	}
	msg := got[0]
	mac := hmac.New(sha256.New, []byte(notifySecret))
	mac.Write([]byte(msg.timestamp + "."))
	mac.Write(msg.body)
	if msg.event != "run.finished" || msg.delivery == "" || msg.signature != "sha256="+hex.EncodeToString(mac.Sum(nil)) {
		s.t.Fatalf("message headers = %+v", msg)
	}
	var ev struct {
		Kind       string   `json:"kind"`
		Categories []string `json:"categories"`
		Schedule   struct {
			Name string `json:"name"`
		} `json:"schedule"`
		Run struct {
			ID          string `json:"id"`
			Result      string `json:"result"`
			Hosts       int    `json:"hosts"`
			HostsFailed int    `json:"hosts_failed"`
		} `json:"run"`
		URL string `json:"url"`
	}
	if err := json.Unmarshal(msg.body, &ev); err != nil {
		s.t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if ev.Schedule.Name != "failing nightly" || ev.Run.Result != "failed" || ev.Run.Hosts != 1 || ev.Run.HostsFailed != 1 ||
		ev.Run.ID != group.Jobs[0].ID || !strings.HasSuffix(ev.URL, "/runs/"+group.Jobs[0].ID) {
		s.t.Fatalf("message = %s", msg.body)
	}
	// Never log output or variables: the playbook's failure text and the
	// template's extra var name stay out of the message.
	if strings.Contains(string(msg.body), "deliberate failure") || strings.Contains(string(msg.body), "should_fail") {
		s.t.Fatalf("the message carries run output or variables: %s", msg.body)
	}
	var log struct {
		Deliveries []struct {
			Status string `json:"status"`
		} `json:"deliveries"`
	}
	s.get(prefix+"/notification-channels/"+ch.Channel.ID+"/deliveries", &log)
	if len(log.Deliveries) != 1 || log.Deliveries[0].Status != "sent" {
		s.t.Fatalf("delivery log = %+v", log.Deliveries)
	}

	// --- A maintenance window leaves a host out of a scheduled run ------------
	var spare hostOut
	s.post(prefix+"/hosts", map[string]any{"name": "e2e-maintained", "zone_id": s.zoneID, "address": "192.0.2.10", "managed": true}, &spare)
	var both templateOut
	s.post(prefix+"/job-templates", map[string]any{
		"name": "hello-both", "playbook_id": s.createEditorPlaybook("hello-both", greetTemplate(s.t)),
		"scope":      map[string]any{"host_ids": []string{s.hostID, spare.Host.ID}},
		"extra_vars": map[string]any{"greeting": s.greeting},
	}, &both)
	now := time.Now().UTC()
	s.post(prefix+"/maintenance-windows", map[string]any{
		"name": "spare host upgrade", "target_kind": "host", "target_id": spare.Host.ID,
		"starts_at": now.Add(-24 * time.Hour).Format(time.RFC3339), "ends_at": now.Add(48 * time.Hour).Format(time.RFC3339),
	}, &map[string]any{})
	var guarded scheduleOut
	s.post(prefix+"/schedules", map[string]any{
		"template_id": both.Template.ID, "name": "guarded", "mode": "cron", "cron": "*/5 * * * *", "timezone": "UTC",
		"guards": map[string]any{"maintenance": "exclude_hosts", "catch_up": map[string]any{"enabled": true, "window": "1h"}},
	}, &guarded)
	if guarded.Schedule.NextRunAt.After(clock.Now()) {
		clock.setAt(*guarded.Schedule.NextRunAt)
	}
	run := s.awaitScheduleRun(guarded.Schedule.ID, 30*time.Second)
	if run.Outcome != "triggered" || !strings.Contains(run.Reason, "1 host(s) left out for maintenance") {
		s.t.Fatalf("guarded run = %+v", run)
	}
	var guardedJobs jobListOut
	s.get(prefix+"/jobs?group="+run.JobGroupID, &guardedJobs)
	if len(guardedJobs.Jobs) != 1 {
		s.t.Fatalf("guarded jobs = %+v", guardedJobs.Jobs)
	}
	done := s.awaitJob(guardedJobs.Jobs[0].ID, "run", 90*time.Second)
	if done.Job.Status != "succeeded" || len(done.Job.Hosts) != 1 || done.Job.Hosts[0] != s.hostID {
		s.t.Fatalf("guarded run = %q on %v, want succeeded on the one host outside the window%s", done.Job.Status, done.Job.Hosts, s.runnerOutput())
	}

	// --- The calendar shows both schedules and the window ----------------------
	q := url.Values{"from": {now.Add(-time.Hour).Format(time.RFC3339)}, "to": {now.Add(7 * 24 * time.Hour).Format(time.RFC3339)}}
	var cal calendarOut
	s.get(prefix+"/calendar?"+q.Encode(), &cal)
	var failedPast, planned bool
	for _, e := range cal.Entries {
		if e.ScheduleID == failing.Schedule.ID && e.State == "triggered" && e.JobStatus == "failed" {
			failedPast = true
		}
		if e.ScheduleID == guarded.Schedule.ID && e.State == "planned" {
			planned = true
		}
	}
	if !failedPast || !planned {
		s.t.Fatalf("calendar entries = %+v, want the failed past run and planned guarded runs", cal.Entries)
	}
	if len(cal.Maintenance) != 1 || cal.Maintenance[0].Name != "spare host upgrade" {
		s.t.Fatalf("calendar maintenance = %+v", cal.Maintenance)
	}
}
```

Notes on the choices in this test:
- The failing schedule is **paused** as soon as its run has finished. The clock moves again for the second schedule, and the first schedule's next `*/5` slot would otherwise fire a second failing run and a second, legitimate message.
- Both schedules enable catch-up (1 h), as in the v0.2c gate. The injected clock can be minutes ahead of the wall clock, so the guarded schedule's first occurrence may already be due when it is created, and a slow tick must not turn it into `missed`.
- The spare host (`192.0.2.10`, TEST-NET-1) is never dialed: the guard leaves it out before the enqueue. If the exclusion broke, the run would target it and fail, and the assertion on the job's hosts would catch it first.
- "Exactly one" waits five seconds after the first message, which is more than two dispatcher intervals, so a duplicate enqueue or a double send would show.

In §7, replace the end of the schedules bullet, "The maintenance-window guard arrives with maintenance windows (v0.2d).", with:

```markdown
  The maintenance guard (v0.2d) is `ignore`, `skip` (record `skipped_maintenance`
```

Replace the bullet `- Schedule notifications (v0.2d): …` with:

```markdown
- Schedule notifications (v0.2d): rules per schedule (`failure`, `change`, `success`,
  `skipped` → channels from §11), stored merged per channel so a run yields at most
  one message per channel. A run group is reported once, from the terminal
  transaction of its last job; `change` means a host reported changed in the stored
  per-host results; a cancelled run counts as a failure. Skipped, missed and failed
  decisions are reported from the ticker. Deliveries are written in the same
  transaction as the fact (idempotent per event and channel) and sent at least once.
  A weekly digest per organization (runs, failures, skips and misses, slowest runs of
  7 days) and a calendar of scheduled runs and maintenance windows
  (`GET /calendar`, at most six weeks) complete it.
```

In §11, replace the "Channels" bullet with:

```markdown
- **Channels**: email (SMTP), generic webhook (HTTP method, headers, body template with
  alert fields), Telegram bot, Slack, Mattermost, ntfy, Pushover, Twilio SMS, PagerDuty
  Events v2. Each channel has a test button. Delivery is a manager-side job queue with
  retries and a per-alert notification log. **v0.2d built email and webhook**: e-mail
  to up to 20 recipients through the instance SMTP; webhooks with sealed URL, header
  values and signing secret, a body template over a fixed event with a `json` helper,
  and `X-Pulsix-Signature-256` over `<timestamp>.<body>`; a synchronous test; a queue
  shared by managers (`SKIP LOCKED`, 2-minute lease, attempts as fencing token, 6
  attempts with 30 s × 4ⁿ backoff, permanent failures not retried) and a per-channel
  log of the newest 500 deliveries.
```

and add to the "Maintenance windows" bullet: "v0.2d built host, inventory-group (subgroups included) and organization windows, one-off or recurring (cron, duration, time zone), used by the schedules' maintenance guard and the calendar; silencing, monitoring-group targets and public windows follow."

- [ ] **Step 5: The architecture spec, §14**

In the "Incoming webhooks (v0.2c)" bullet, replace "Outbound webhooks (notification channels, with SSRF rules) are v0.2d." with "Outbound webhooks follow below." After that bullet, insert:

```markdown
- **Outbound webhooks (v0.2d).** Notification webhooks are requests to addresses
  operators enter, so every one goes through one client (`internal/outbound`): the
  name is resolved first and the connection dialled to the checked address, so a
  second DNS answer cannot slip in; a destination is refused as a whole when any of
  its addresses is loopback, link-local (metadata `169.254.169.254` included),
  unspecified, multicast, broadcast, `100.100.100.200` or `fd00:ec2::254`, unless
  `PULSIX_NOTIFY_ALLOW_CIDRS` re-admits the range; private ranges are allowed;
  redirects are not followed, proxy variables are ignored, and every request ends
  after 10 s. The URL (it can carry a token), header values and the signing secret
  are sealed with AAD `channel:<org>:<id>`; responses show scheme and host only, and
  transport errors are stored without the URL. Messages carry names, results, counts,
  durations and links — never run output, variables or secrets.
```

- [ ] **Step 6: The design spec's implementation notes**

Append to `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-18-v0.2c-automation-design.md`:

```markdown
## 12. v0.2d implementation notes

Recorded while planning v0.2d (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-19-v0.2d-notifications.md`),
where the implementation departs from or sharpens §5–§8:

- `maintenance_windows` stores `host_id` and `group_id` (composite foreign keys, cascade)
  instead of one `target_id`; the API keeps `target_kind` + `target_id`.
- `notify_rules` are stored merged per channel (`schedule_notifications`) and exposed as
  the sub-resource `GET/PUT /schedules/{id}/notifications` in the `{on, channels}` shape,
  not as a field of the schedule body.
- A webhook's URL is sealed like its header values (Slack-style URLs carry a token).
- "Send test" sends synchronously and records the attempt in the delivery log.
- A cancelled run counts as `failure`. Run now applies no guard (overlap included).
- The outbound refusals add unspecified, multicast and broadcast addresses and two more
  metadata addresses; a name with any refused address is refused as a whole.
- An e-mail channel sends one message to all its recipients.
- 4xx answers other than 408/429 fail a delivery at once; a digest more than 24 h late
  is skipped.
```

After the "v0.2c follow-ups" table, add:

```markdown
## v0.2d follow-ups

Decisions and limits recorded while planning v0.2d (2026-09-19).

| # | Item | Status | Notes |
|---|------|--------|-------|
| 2.72 | Notification rules for webhook-triggered and manual runs | idea | v0.2d rules hang off schedules only (spec §7); a webhook trigger or a template could carry the same `{on, channels}` rules |
| 2.73 | "Notify" column in the schedules list | idea | the mockup's list shows each schedule's rules; v0.2d shows them in the schedule panel only |
| 2.74 | Per-recipient e-mail delivery | idea | an e-mail channel sends one message to all recipients (they see each other), and a retry after a partial SMTP failure resends to everyone |
| 2.75 | Resend and payload view in the delivery log | idea | the log shows status, attempts and the last error; operators cannot resend a failed message or see what was sent |
| 2.76 | NAT64 and other embedded-IPv4 destinations in the outbound policy | idea | `64:ff9b::/96` and similar prefixes that embed a loopback or metadata IPv4 address are not unwrapped before the check |
| 2.77 | Calendar in the schedule's or organization's time zone | idea | the SPA lays the calendar out in the browser's time zone; a team spread over zones may want a fixed one |
| 2.78 | Playwright specs for the notifications, maintenance and calendar pages | planned | Vitest and the Go E2E cover them today (like 2.68 for v0.2c) |
```

**User-requested decisions, where they are made:** the run-finished source is decided under "Decisions" and built in Task 5. Change detection: "Decisions", Task 5. The digest scheduler and its idempotency: "Decisions", Task 10. Delivery idempotency and restarts: "Decisions", Task 3. Message content never including logs, variables or secrets: Global Constraints, the `Event` struct in Task 2, and the E2E assertion in Task 12.

**Placeholder scan.** No "TBD", "TODO", "implement later" or "similar to Task N" remain. Every code step carries the code, and every run step names the command and the expected result.

**Type and name consistency** (checked across tasks):
- `jobs.RunGroupResult`, `jobs.RunGroupSink`, `SQLQueue.SetRunGroupSink` (Task 5), used by `notify.Outbox.RunGroupFinished` (Task 9) and the app wiring (Task 9).
- `jobs.RunRequest.ExcludeHosts`, `jobs.ErrAllHostsExcluded`, `jobs.Service.RunTargets` (Task 5), used by `schedules` (Task 8).
- `jobs.GroupSummary`, `Service.GroupSummaries` (Task 5), used by `schedules.Service.Digest` (Task 10) and `calendar` (Task 11).
- `maintenance.Service.HostsInMaintenance` / `schedules.MaintenanceChecker` (Tasks 6, 8), `maintenance.Service.OccurrencesBetween` / `calendar.Windows` (Tasks 6, 11), `app.NewGroupHosts` (Task 7, also in `apitest`).
- `notify.Deps` grows in Tasks 3 (`Deliveries`, `Sender`, `PublicURL`), 9 (`Rules`) and 10 (`Digests`). The app and `apitest` literals are extended in the same tasks.
- `notify.Decision` and `schedules.DecisionOutbox` (Task 9). `notify.DigestBuilder` is implemented by `schedules.Service.Digest` (Task 10).
- `schedules.Service.RunsBetween` (Task 10), used by `calendar.ScheduleSource` (Task 11).
- SPA: `useChannels` (Task 4) is used by `ScheduleNotifications` (Task 9) and `DigestCard` (Task 10). `NotifyChannel` (Task 4) is used by `DigestCard`. The runs route search `{status, kind, trigger, schedule}` is set in Task 11 in the router, the shell, the run view and the runs page.

**Found during the dry run and folded into the tasks.** The whole plan was implemented in a scratch copy of `main` 1ab4c33 before writing. Every code block here is taken from that copy. The Go suite, `golangci-lint`, the SPA's `tsc`/`eslint`/Vitest (401 tests) and both E2E gates passed there.
- Template names must match `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9._-]{0,62}$`: the E2E's second template is `hello-both`.
- sqlc types `COALESCE(h.name, g.name, '')::text` as a non-null `string`, and `gen.GetMaintenanceWindowRow` converts to `gen.ListMaintenanceWindowsRow`.
- `min()`/`max()` over nullable timestamps need raw SQL scanning into pointers (`GroupSummaries`, the run-group query). The worker writes JSON `null`, not `[]`, for empty host lists, so the run-group query guards with `jsonb_typeof`.
- A duplicate insert inside a transaction aborts the transaction, so the digest's channel list is de-duplicated in the service, not by catching the unique violation.
- huma answers enum violations with 422 and the service's own validation with 400. The tests assert each exactly.
- A queued job cancelled last completes its run group outside `terminate`, so `ClearTerminalPayload` re-checks the group under the same lock.
- `internal/jobs` coverage sat at exactly 90.0 % until the cancel-path and run-target error tests were added (90.3 %).
- `unused` counts test usage, so the render and category helpers of Task 2 lint clean before their production callers exist.
- The docs' body-template example had to guard every section with `with`: a digest carries no `.Schedule`.
